**5.5.4(b)** (G4.7(b), changes). *What changed*: late per the keepers O names, and per the clock O names; "No other anchoring gives the Terms' own answer". *The exchanges*: "The place vouches for the chaining only" (26 November); "Ana's month" (27 November): "The clock specified in the agreement and its anchoring."; "Whose place counts" (26 November). The lead's reading on G1.4, reaching it (section 6).

**5.5.4(c)** (G4.7(c), changes). *What changed*: the answer is **late**, qualified as shown, per keeper or per clock; where a chain that could show L late needs an act named in a field and not held, whether L is late is **unchecked**. *The exchange*: "A missing act stays unchecked: no door around it" (26 November, 18:34–18:57), under 5.13.3. The brief read draft 25's "might be what shows L came late" two ways; under "Knots are knots" the two come to one (the outline, section 10, row 4). "Knots against a keeper" is the lead's reading of 10:07 (section 6).

**5.5.5** (G4.8, changes). *What changed*: a Terms act replaces the original "only as 5.5.1 says: by the gate, and the pin"; draft 25's sentence handing "in force" to its first-version rule goes. *The exchanges*: as 5.5.1. He: "Ok, that”s what I was trying to remember we forced the deadlock and push them to resolve it".

**5.5.5(a)** (G4.8(a), changes). *What changed*: the default signers are the known parties, "the author among them … never a party named by role, and never a signer who fills a role"; "the default asks for no one's signature" goes. *The exchanges*: "Who signs an update by default: the known parties" (26 November, 17:51–17:56), marked "the MOR lead's reading" in the ledger. He: "It’s the protect the unknown party expressed the old way." The known parties come from his earlier "only the known parties can" (15:52); his 17:56 words name no default signers (item 33, section 6). And "There is no unsigned act" (27 November): "There is no such thing as an unsigned act on Mor"; the lead's line: "the default signers of any update of Terms are the known parties, the author among them. Draft 25's "the default asks for no one's signature" (G4.8(a)) goes." Which Terms "when made" fix the known parties, and whether a party a later version adds is known, were left out (item 2), and are written in wave four (below).

**5.5.5(a), and §3.2 Known party** (wave four, N1). *What changed*: "of a version, a signer it names: a party it names as a signer, or an agreement it names as a party, as the version names it, which signs as its own Terms require (§5.1); never one who takes part through a role. The known parties are read version by version (wave five's words, P1-5, on the lead's reading C14) … A party that an update names, and the version it replaces does not, is a known party from that update on, and is not one of the known parties of the versions before it" (§3.2); in 5.5.5(a), the default signers are "the known parties of the original", and "A party the update names that the original does not is not among them: it is a known party from the update on (§3.2)"; 5.5.5(b) and 5.5.7(e) agree. *The exchange*: "The known parties of a version are the signers it names" (27 November, 16:44–16:46), the brief's item 2. The MOR lead asked whether Dana, added as a named party by an update the known parties sign, is known from then on, with a say in later updates, leaning yes. He: "Is Dana a known party at the signing of the new act?" The MOR lead: yes; one rule, version by version: the known parties of a version are the signers it names. He: "Yes". *The lead's line*: "the known parties of a version are the signers it names, read version by version. A party a valid update adds is known from that version on, having signed it; she was unknown to the versions before it." Why: what makes a party known is that the version names her when it is made and she signs it; she has no say in the update that adds her, being named by it, not by the original (scenario 8.23). Draft 26 keeps "as a signer or as an agreement", on the lead's reading of 09:47 that an agreement named as a party is a default signer (section 6.6); the line says "the signers it names" (the trace, N1's rows to check).

*The found item, answered for the companion*: an agreement named as a party that has never signed. The lead's reading (09:47): "it is a known party and among the default signers; how it signs is its own rule's (G1), and an update it does not sign is not valid by default." Draft 26 writes it in 5.5.5(a). What follows from it (the scribe's reading): such a party is not counted for a first version's coming into force (5.4.4), and is needed for every default update; Terms that name an agreement as a party and want updates without it name an update condition.

**5.5.5(d)** (G4.8(d), changes). *What changed*: judged per the keepers the original names; "an order counts once each of them shows it, and where they agree; until each has shown it, it is **not shown**, with that reason; where they disagree, it is **not shown**, with the reason that the keepers disagree, and the disagreement is kept as evidence" (wave four). *The exchange*: "The place vouches for the chaining only", its addition of 17:49. He: "Two disagreeing has its value though". The lead's line: "two places that disagree are evidence in themselves, one lying or broken, shown by the signed records … a forced deadlock like a fork; the cost, two dependencies". Whether one silent keeper stops the order is left out (item 5).

**5.5.6** (G4.9, changes). *What changed*: Terms stop only when an update that replaces them is in force (5.5.2); a version that dissolves an agreement is such an update, "by the update condition of the version it names, as that condition's cMIP dictates"; "A version that cannot be updated is one whose update condition answers not valid to every update". *The exchanges*: "Leaving takes two acts" (18 November): "Wait… an agreement can always end, it is not a feature, it is an update to the agreement that states the dissolution of it"; "What a dissolution is" (18 November): "The parties remain, they sign the dissolution. The powers are gone, no more action is possible. What it offers is gone, there is no more offer. What it says, previous agreement not in force anymore as agreed by all parties. Cannot be updated." and "The update condition decides, as dictated by the cMIP that governs it. My line was a loose example". The lead's reading on G4.9 and G4.11(i) (section 6). His list is not written as a rule: "My line was a loose example", and the grammar reads every version alike. Kept from draft 25: "a verifier MUST NOT answer that Terms or an agreement have ended" (an agreement "only gets new versions"). What a verifier shows of an agreement dissolved among the known parties while a filling's version still binds is left out (item 13).

**5.5.7(a)** (G4.11(a), changes). *What changed*: a condition that needs a clock or a keeper "is given one only where the Terms name it: Terms that do not supply what the condition's cMIP declares it needs (a clock, a keeper) are **invalid**"; draft 25's "or of the anchoring named where it names none" goes. *The exchange*: "A condition in time with no anchor is refused" (26 November), under 5.8.6. Its wording is the lead's reading of 10:07, row 6 (section 6).

**5.5.7(c)** (G4.11(c), changes). *What changed*: the ground of not valid is an act, or "a time, per the clock the Terms name"; "A time has no other ground". *The exchange*: as 5.5.7(a). He (16:33, under ""Before the close" was the simulation's"): "But let’s answer it… an agreement with no stated anchor cannot build a temporal condition".

**5.5.7(d)** (G4.11(d), changes). *What changed*: "Valid is the gate's answer only: the update replaces the version it names once its order is also shown"; draft 25's valid meant "replaces". *The exchange*: "Valid and in force, two words, two jobs" (26 November, 18:00). He: "Agreed." The lead's line: "This refines his line of 26 November ("in force to be human speech, and valid to be its translation"): both stay, each with its own job." His earlier line: "Yes, I always assumed in force to be human speech, and valid to be its translation for the verifier to be honest".

**5.5.7(e)** (G4.11(e), changes). *What changed*: the default: "an update is valid when every known party of those Terms signs it", "never a signer who fills a role"; draft 25's sentence on a delegation goes. *The exchanges*: as 5.5.5(a); "Delegation is a leftover" (18 November).

**5.5.7(f)** (G4.11(f), changes). *What changed*: "What a filling gave lasts as long as the words of the version it was earned under say, counted on the clock that version names … no update ends it, and none is needed"; draft 25's "What a version offers changes only through an update" no longer holds for a filling. *The exchange*: "Ana's month ends by the clock the agreement names" (27 November): "There is no valid update to sign, only signed terms to respect." "the clock that version names" is the lead's reading of 13:20 (section 6).

**5.5.7(g)** (G4.11(g), changes). *What changed*: by default a resolving update of Terms asks for the known parties, no longer "everyone who signed the shared version"; its last sentence, history, comes to section 11. *The exchange*: "Who signs an update by default" (26 November). Whether a resolving update must itself be pinned is left out (item 10).

**5.5.7(i)** (G4.11(i), changes). *What changed*: "An update condition whose cMIP answers not valid to every update is read as any other: the version that names it cannot be updated"; draft 25's "and in G10.6" goes with G10.6(b). *The exchanges*: "A buyer under a replaced version" (26 November): "Yes, it was a bad idea."; "What a dissolution is" (18 November): "Cannot be updated." The lead's reading on G4.9 and G4.11(i) (section 6).

**Wave five, the readings carried** (P1's edits, and P2's in encoding 3; every row the MOR lead's, `docs/lead/notes/2026-11-27-draft-26-readings.md`, in full in section 6.14). One line for each row, under the rule it shaped first:

- **C3 (an update's pin: its own knots, or the acts that put it through the gate)**: "Either: the update's own knots, or those of the acts that put it through the gate. (ii)." *Shaped*: 5.5.1(b) (P1-20).
- **F4 (the acts that put a version in force: completing assents, or pin acts too)**: "For 5.5.4(a), the acts enough for the gate; the pin is not part of lateness. For 5.5.1(b), the update is pinned by its own knots or by those acts' (C3)." *Shaped*: 5.5.1(b) (P1-20), 5.5.4 and its (a), (b) (P1-22 to 24, P1-61), §3.3 **Late update** (P1-9).
- **C4 (is "valid" answered of an update whose original is not in force)**: "Valid, since the gate asks only whether the rule allows it; in force waits for the original. Two answers, "valid" and "not in force: <reason>"." *Shaped*: 5.5.1, "Valid, and in force" (P1-21).
- **C46a ("valid, not in force": two answers)**: "Two answers, the second with its reason." *Shaped*: 5.5.1, "Valid, and in force" (P1-21).
- **C17, F2 (5.5.4(a), one act or several together)**: "Together. L is late when no choice of assents enough for L's gate avoids an act shown after acts enough for F's gate. Read act by act, a two-of-three update with three assents could never be late." *Shaped*: 5.5.4(a) (P1-23, P1-62).
- **C46b (F's own pin left out of L's test)**: "Yes: lateness is against the gate, not the pin." *Shaped*: 5.5.4 (P1-22 to 24, P1-61), §3.3 **Late update** (P1-9).
- **C28 (a keeper that contradicts a knot)**: "Only the answers resting on that record are "not shown, the keeper contradicts the acts"; the record is kept as evidence." *Shaped*: 5.5.4(c) (P1-25), 5.8.4 (P1-37).
- **F3 (5.5.5(a)'s "in order" sentence)**: "It is one case of 5.5.4(a), not a second test: a second assent naming the first is a knot that may show the second update late by 5.5.4(a)." *Shaped*: 5.5.5(a) (P1-26).
- **C46c ("two assents, same previous": the line's fork only)**: "The line's fork only (5.7.2); the gate makes no test of its own." *Shaped*: 5.5.5(a) (P1-27).

### §5.6 Who is bound

*Draft 25's lines* (G5), rewritten, the scribe's, where words changed. *Lie exposed* (draft 25): ""She bound you", "the group decided", "I was still authorised", said without a power the person bound had signed for; and "we have the power, so the rules are changed", or "so there is a new judge", said by those who may act under the Terms and may not update them; and "my agent's signature does not count any more", said by a grantor to someone who agreed to what the agent signed." This still holds; *added*: "the Terms changed, so you are bound", said to the holder of a filling (5.6.1(c)). *Protects* (draft 25): "Whoever is bound through someone else (the delegator, the outvoted member, the absent party), and whoever relies on an agent, who keeps what the agent's act gave them, as under any update." *Rewritten*: "the grantor" for "the delegator", the word delegation having gone; and the holder of a filling, whom no update binds.

**5.6.1(b)** (G5.1(b), changes). *What changed*: a power binds a person only where "their own signature is on the version of those Terms that gives the power, the version `under` names, as an assent counted in their name (5.6.5(f)), and they are a known party of that version: no update condition can give a power for a person who did not sign the version that gives it, and an assent that fills an unknown party's spot gives no power; for a filling's holder, an act made under a power counts as their signature only where they gave that power in an act of their own (5.10.7)" (wave five: "the version `under` names" is the lead's reading C18; the rest is "A filling gives no power", section 5, "Wave five"). *The exchanges*: "Nothing about me without my signature" (18 November), under 5.1.2; "A power given binds its giver" (18 November): "If you grant powers via a cMIP you take on the responsibilities". The pointer to 5.6.5(f) is the lead's reading on G5.6(f) (section 6).

**5.6.1(c)** (G5.1(c), changes). *What changed*: one is bound by a Terms act one did not sign only "as a known party", and only where it passes the gate and the pin; "No update binds anyone through an unknown party's filling (§3.2)" (wave five, P1-56: his correction "“that fills an unknown party spot”"). *The exchanges*: "For the unknown party there is no update" (27 November): "There is no valid update for her anymore. Only a valid agreement, this is because she is the unknown party the network protects. No?"; "A buyer under a replaced version" (26 November, 15:52): "she has no say in the updating of it, only the known parties can"; "The gate and the pin" (26 November). "also passes the pin" is the brief's reading. Whether (c) stays at all was asked (the trace, M5's rows to check, 1): read as the assignment's plain words ("never by an update one did not sign"), (c) and 5.6.2's second way go, and a two-of-three update condition would bind no third party; draft 26 is written as the brief and the outline lay it out.

**5.6.1(a) and 5.6.3** (wave four, N1). *What changed*: 5.6.1(a) gains "(an act made for another under a power is not made in its signer's own name: 5.6.3)"; 5.6.3 gains "An act with `for` and `under` is made in the name its `for` names, and binds only as 5.6.1(b) says: it binds the grantor (5.6.6), who gave the power, and never its signer, the agent, as an act of the agent's own. [Wave five adds, P1-30: "A power a version carries is given by that version's known parties, and binds only them (5.6.1(b))."] So it is where the grantor is an agreement acting through powers its Terms give (5.1.2): the agreement is bound, as its Terms say, and the signer who acted for it is not bound by that act as an act of its own." *The exchange*: "The giver bears it, not the agent" (27 November, 16:50–16:53), the second question carried to the end of stage two (the scribe's M5; the blind reader's gap, M5). The MOR lead: Ben signs an assent for Ana under a power she gave; Ana is bound; is Ben bound too; leaned no, not by the grammar. He: "No, Ana took the risk, she bears the responsability. It’s my “blame the manager not the worker” mentality. What will be more likely is acting for an “agreement as a party” via granted powers, there two the agreement bears the responsability". *The lead's line*: "an act signed for another under a power binds the giver of the power, never the agent: the giver took the risk and bears the responsibility. The same where the giver is an agreement acting as a party through granted powers, the likelier case: the agreement bears it. The agent's own duties toward the giver are the power's cMIP's, above the core." *Not in the text*: the agent's duties toward the giver are the power's cMIP's (task K3), above the core; no grammar rule reads them. Where the agent is also a party bound by the agreement it acts for, it is bound as that agreement's Terms bind their parties, and not by the act as its own (the scribe's reading of "the agreement bears the responsability"; the trace, N1's rows to check). Scenario 8.26.

**5.6.2** (G5.2, changes). *What changed*: the binding of 5.6.1(c) no longer reaches a filling; "Under the default every known party has signed the update"; draft 25's sentences on draft 18's power for replacement come to section 11. *The exchanges*: "For the unknown party there is no update" (27 November); "Only rules in the hashed text" (17 November). The ground draft 25 gave for the binding (G5.2, carried into 5.6.2 and moved out of the text by M11, below): a known party "signed, or was bound by, Terms that say how they may be updated, and it is bound by an update made so".

**Moved from 5.6.2 by M11** (check 3, only rules: a reason, not a rule). Draft 25's G5.2 and draft 26's 5.6.2 said why a known party is bound by an update it did not sign: "That party signed, or was bound by, Terms that say how they may be updated, and it is bound by an update made so." The rule stays in 5.6.1(c) and 5.6.2; the reason is here.

**5.6.4** (G5.4, changes). *What changed*: an act under a power whose Terms were replaced binds where it was made before the replacing Terms passed "the gate and the pin", per the keepers those Terms name, or by knots; where nothing shows their order the act is "**not shown** as binding through those Terms, and nothing more: the act and the replacing Terms are no fork of the versions (§5.12)" (wave five, P1-31, the lead's readings C19 and C12/F12; N1's text had "is not said by this rule"). *The exchanges*: "The gate and the pin" (26 November); "The place vouches for the chaining only" (26 November); "A fork is a fork" (27 November). The brief read the unordered case two ways (draft 25's "not shown", or a fork); N1 wrote only what both agree on (item 8, left out); wave five writes the first, on the lead's reading (section 6.14).

**5.6.5(a)** (G5.6(a), changes). *What changed*: with no update condition named, the constitution is "an update every known party has signed", passed by the gate and the pin. *The exchange*: "Who signs an update by default" (26 November): "It’s the protect the unknown party expressed the old way." The rule's ground is draft 25's quotation of him, "Update equals constitution, and the judicial, power means the executional", given in the text's own words.

**5.6.6** (G5.7, changes). *What changed*: "the delegator of a delegation" becomes "the one who gave the power" (the **grantor**); "a filling is governed by the version its proof names, which no update reaches"; "bound by an update they did not sign" reaches known parties only. *The exchanges*: "Delegation is a leftover" (18 November); "Who signs an update by default" (26 November); "A buyer under a replaced version" (26 November); "For the unknown party there is no update" (27 November). The rule's ground is draft 25's quotation of him: "It’s never set aside, it’s always update. Whoever agreed to what was signed by the agent is still covered, the same way as any updates of terms."

**5.6.6(a)** (G5.7(a), changes). *What changed*: a power ends by a version of the Terms that give it, "their update condition decides, as its cMIP dictates", or "as the power's own rule says, which answers when the power ends (task K3)"; draft 25's pointer to G5.5 goes. *The exchanges*: "Delegation is a leftover" (18 November): "How does the person give the power away? To assume that there is no way out is to assume a very specific shape for the cMIp"; "Firing an employee" (18 November): "True, it is now a set of agreements, no a collective having its rules, so each agreement is separate, one is the collective, one is the employment". The lead's reading on G4.9 and G4.11(i), reaching it (section 6).

**5.6.6(b)** (G5.7(b), changes). *What changed*: the default is every known party; draft 25's "and so is everyone who agreed to what the agent signed" goes with the old default; the agent's assent counts as the grantor's (5.6.5(f)). *The exchanges*: "Who signs an update by default" (26 November); "There is no unsigned act" (27 November): "Sounds right."

**5.6.6(c)** (G5.7(c), changes). *What changed*: a counter signature in the grantor's name is available "until the assents that put the Terms in force are shown before it"; a later one, or one once the Terms are in force, changes nothing. *The exchanges*: "In force once pinned" (18 November): "Agreed. Let them get stuck and cook it clean"; "A fork is a fork" (27 November). The ruling on item 1 and the reading of 09:47, row 2 (section 6).

**Wave five, the readings carried** (P1's edits, and P2's in encoding 3; every row the MOR lead's, `docs/lead/notes/2026-11-27-draft-26-readings.md`, in full in section 6.14). One line for each row, under the rule it shaped first:

- **C18 (which version gives a power)**: "The version `under` names." *Shaped*: 5.6.1(b) (P1-29).
- **C19 (an act under a power against the replacing Terms, unordered)**: "Not shown as binding; not a fork of the versions." *Shaped*: 5.6.4 (P1-31).
- **C12, F12 (5.6.4's unordered case; a contest's version)**: "Not shown, nothing more; a contest needs its Terms shown as a version, in force or not." *Shaped*: 5.6.4 (P1-31), 5.11.8 (P1-45).

### §5.7 A signer's own line

*Draft 25's lines* (G6), still holding: *Lie exposed*: "One history shown to one person and another to another." *Protects*: "The stranger who is shown only one branch." No rule of §5.7 changed: 5.7.1 and 5.7.2 stay, on the lead's reading on G6.1 and G6.2 (section 6). The strangers' "A fork convicts itself" is the ground (section 1).

**Moved from 5.7.2 by M11** (check 3, only rules: a reason, not a rule). Draft 25's G6.2 and draft 26's 5.7.2 said why a tip the keepers do not show is still a tip: "so that no signer can hide a fork by keeping one tip away from the keepers". The rule stays in 5.7.2; the reason is here.

**Two lines** (5.7.3 of M5's draft, sent here by the lead's reading of 10:07, row 5: "5.7.3 to the companion"). Its content, as M5 wrote it: "`previous` is the agreement's thread, per signer: the line of one signer's acts within one agreement, which these grammar rules define. `prev`, the line of all a signer's acts across their life, is the layer below's (Envelopes, Identity): these grammar rules read it and define nothing of it. Each is a field, and each fingerprint either carries is a knot (§3.4, §5.13). An author's first act within the agreement is the Terms act they signed (§4.3)." It rests on "Two threads, two owners" (17 November): the MOR lead set out the two threads every act sits on and leaned to keep both, "since they catch different lies". He: "It does". The lead's line: "both threads stay. The signer's line is the lower layer's (Identity and the act format); the agreement's thread is Agreements'. A key change continues the signer's line; it never starts a fresh one." Draft 26 says the same in §2 (Identity), §3.3 (Line) and §4.3 (an author's first act), so the sentence is explanation, not a rule. Its encoding half (E23's test of "the same agreement") went to encoding 3 by the same reading.

**Wave five, the readings carried** (P1's edits, and P2's in encoding 3; every row the MOR lead's, `docs/lead/notes/2026-11-27-draft-26-readings.md`, in full in section 6.14). One line for each row, under the rule it shaped first:

- **F7 (two acts, same `previous`, ordered by `prev`)**: "A fork of the agreement line (5.7.2); `prev` orders them for "shown after" but the line is still forked, and the signer resolves it by a resolving act." *Shaped*: 5.7.2 (P1-32), §3.3 **Fork** (P1-10).
- **C30 (a forked assent and a first version)**: "Neither tip counts for the coming into force either." *Shaped*: 5.7.2 (P1-33).

### §5.8 The keeper, and the clock

*Draft 25's lines* (G7), rewritten, the scribe's. *Lie exposed* (draft 25): ""There is nothing later", "there is no side piece", "nobody else holds it"." *Rewritten*: and "this came after that", said by a keeper the Terms name, against the acts: the keeper's record contradicts a knot, and its lie is signed (5.8.4). *Protects* (draft 25): "Everyone who arrives later and must rely on "nothing else exists"." *Rewritten*: and, where several keepers are named, everyone shown one keeper's order: a disagreement is shown as such, both records kept (5.8.3).

**5.8.1** (G7.1, changes; with N2, new). *What changed*: `place` becomes `keeper`, one or several: "the agreement's official relay: the way the drafters of the agreement agree on where it is kept"; it "relays the agreement's acts as any relay does, and vouches for their order only"; "It never vouches for an act's validity". *The exchanges*: "The place vouches for the chaining only" (26 November) and "Agreements gets an introduction" (27 November): section 4.6, 4.2 and 4.4.

**5.8.2** (N2). *What it says*: copies may be lodged at any relay; "Only the order given by the keepers the Terms name counts". *The exchange*: "The place vouches for the chaining only": "redundancy can also live elsewhere"; the lead's line: "a mirror keeping acts and giving no second order".

**5.8.3** (N2; wave four, N1). *What it says*: where the Terms name several keepers, every keeper named is needed: an act counts for order only once each shows it, and that order only where they agree; until each has shown it, **not shown**, with that reason; where they disagree, **not shown**, the keepers disagree, with the records of each as evidence; two contradicting answers of one keeper give no answer from it, **unchecked**. *The exchanges*: "The place vouches for the chaining only", its addition of 17:49: "Two disagreeing has its value though", "Yes, they should. Can we find a better name for “place”?" The ledger's "not shown" is used where the settled note's M12 puts "unchecked" (item 25: the ledger wins). And "Two keepers named, both needed" (27 November, 16:40–16:44), the brief's item 5: the MOR lead asked whether, where the Terms name two keepers and only one has shown an act, with no disagreement, the act is pinned or waits for both, leaning that it waits. He: "If the terms stipulate two keepers, both keepers are needed. Sorry can’t do". *The lead's line*: "where Terms name several keepers, every keeper named must show an act for its order to count. Until each has, the answer is "not shown", with that reason ("sorry, can't do"). Where they disagree, "not shown, the keepers disagree", as on 26 November." Why: the drafters named both as where the agreement is kept; an order one of them has not given is not the agreement's order. 5.8.4, 5.5.5(d), 5.6.4, §3.4 **Keeper** and **Placed** and §3.6 **Not shown** agree. Scenario 8.22; the variant of 8.4.

**5.8.4** (G7.2, changes). *What changed*: an act counts for order "only once it is placed (§3.4), at the position the keepers give it"; draft 25's list ("what is whole, who holds what, whether a period has passed, or whether something was made public") is left out (item 6, section 4.6); knots "do not pin, or order the agreement's acts, in the keepers' place, but where 5.5.1(b) says so; they expose a keeper" ("but where 5.5.1(b) says so": wave five, P1-37, "A dead keeper"). *The exchanges*: "The place is a pointer" (17 November): "It's just a pointer to a relay then. Why the need for more grammar?"; the lead's line: "where the Terms name a place, every reader takes what is latest from it and from nothing else". The knots clause is the lead's reading of 10:07, row 2 (section 6).

**5.8.5** (G7.4, changes). *What changed*: "place" becomes keepers; the clock the Terms name stays, for every answer that turns on a time; a keeper or anchoring of the reader's own choosing, "or one named only by a rule the parties never signed, gives an answer only as that reader's". *The exchanges*: "Whose anchoring wins" (13 November), as the trace cites it: "Whatever the agreement states as reference clock is the clock everyone has to use for that agreement."; "Whose place counts" (26 November); "Ana's month" (27 November): "The clock specified in the agreement and its anchoring." How Terms name their clock is left out (item 34(a)): the `clock` field is used as draft 25 had it.

**5.8.6** (N9, new). *What it says*: "A condition in time with no anchor the Terms state is refused, not answered": where the bytes show it, Terms that do not supply what a cMIP they name declares it needs (a clock, a keeper) are invalid; "A keeper is not a clock"; where only the words show it, no grammar rule answers. *The exchanges*: ""Before the close" was the simulation's" (26 November, 16:28–16:33). He: "That was a simulation artifact, they had numbered windows", then (16:33): "But let’s answer it… an agreement with no stated anchor cannot build a temporal condition". And "A condition in time with no anchor is refused" (26 November, 18:00–18:30): the MOR lead set Fable's case, Ana's "one month" under Terms naming no clock, "which no verifier could ever end". He: "Yes, condition is missing, it should be refused"; asked who refuses, the grammar not reading words: "Tools that verify that text and agreement state the same thing will be needed". *The lead's line*: "A verifier refuses where the bytes show it … Where only the words show it, a client refuses to sign (client conformance). Tools that check that the words (the Text act) and the agreement's fields say the same thing will be needed; they are built above the core." *The client half* is client conformance and not in draft 26: a client refuses to sign Terms whose words speak of a time with no anchor the Terms state (M10's "for Development"). The entry stands twice in the ledger; the six places where the two copies of the lead's line differ are not used (item 35). "Terms that do not supply what a cMIP … declares it needs" and "A keeper is not a clock" are the lead's reading of 10:07, row 6; how a cMIP declares a need is open (item 34(b), §7).

**Wave five, the readings carried** (P1's edits, and P2's in encoding 3; every row the MOR lead's, `docs/lead/notes/2026-11-27-draft-26-readings.md`, in full in section 6.14). One line for each row, under the rule it shaped first:

- **C13, F16, S5 (several keepers, one silent)**: "Every keeper named must show it, or "not shown"; the answer changes only when the silent keeper shows it, or a version every needed signer signs replaces the keepers." *Shaped*: 5.8.3 (P1-35).
- **C46d (two keepers agree on A and B, disagree on C)**: "Only answers resting on C are not shown." *Shaped*: 5.8.3 (P1-34).
- **C21, C22 (a cMIP's declared needs; a cMIP not held)**: "Declared in the cMIP's own published text, in the form Development fixes; read by the grammar verifier from that act; a cMIP not held leaves 5.8.6 unchecked." *Shaped*: 5.8.6 (P1-38), §6 opening (P1-53), §7 (P1-54); encoding 3, section 9, step 7.7, and section 11, item 11 (P2).

### §5.9 The settlement

No rule changed: 5.9.1 is G8.5 as draft 25 has it. Draft 25's G8 lines ("A claim passed off as a fact: "it was delivered", "you were paid", on the word of the one who gains by it"; protecting "The receiving side, who did not choose to be declared paid or served, and every reader of a verifier's answer") and its G9 lines (""My software says it checks out", from software that could not check"; protecting "Anyone relying on someone else's verification") go with the rules that moved to the MICS ("How a reader answers goes to the MICS", 18 November: "The MICS, I assume they are overarching rules"); M10 lists them under "for the MICS".

### §5.10 Spots, and the unknown party

*Draft 25's lines* (G10), rewritten, the scribe's. *Lie exposed* (draft 25): ""I am the one who did it", confirmed by someone chosen afterwards; and "the terms have changed, so what you earned is gone"." *Rewritten*: and "the terms have changed, so your spot, your place or your term is gone", said to the holder of a filling; and "your month is over", said on a clock her version does not name. *Protects* (draft 25): "Whoever earns a spot and was not there to sign, who keeps exactly what was offered when they earned it." *Rewritten*: who keeps exactly what was offered, for as long as the words of that version say, on its own clock, whatever later version is in force: the unknown party (section 3).

**5.10.5** (G10.5, changes). *What changed*: whether a spot still takes new identities is its cMIP's answer from the version in force; "A condition in time in that answer is answered per the clock the Terms in force name, and for a filling already earned per the clock its own version names (5.8.5, 5.10.2): an anchoring of the reader's own choosing never gives the Terms' answer" (as M11's edit (d) has it); draft 25's "per the anchoring named" goes; "a filling already earned … keeps the rights of the version it was earned under" stands again. *The exchanges*: "A condition in time with no anchor is refused" (26 November): "Yes, condition is missing, it should be refused"; "A buyer under a replaced version" (26 November). The reader's anchoring clause is the lead's reading on G1.4, reaching it (section 6).

**5.10.6** (N5, new). *What it says*: "For an unknown party's filling (§3.2), no later update exists; each later version exists for the agreement. No update of the known parties reaches a filling in any way, and no power a version carries is used for its holder but one they gave in an act of their own (5.10.7)" (M10's edit (j), with wave five's P1-40 and P1-41). *The exchanges*: section 3 in full. "in any way" is the lead's line of 27 November, as the assignment asked it written; the line's list ("her rights, her spot, her place in the agreement"; "close the offer, or dissolve it among themselves") is not written (items 29, 13).

**5.10.6, renewal** (wave four, N1: no sentence added). *The exchange*: ""Renewed on the terms then current" cannot be built" (27 November, 16:46–16:48), the brief's item 14 and the scribe's M28. The MOR lead asked whether such words in the version Ana buys are her own deal, bought knowingly, or a later version reaching her by a back door; leaned her own deal. He: "How did they build such terms in agreements?" The MOR lead: they cannot; the grammar reads Ana under the version her proof names and nothing else; the only way to build renewal is that her month ends on the clock and she assents again, a new filling under the version then in force. He: "Yes, it’s the scenario we trying to make difficult on the “walls of terms”". *The lead's line*: "a filling cannot be moved to a later version by its words: "renews on the terms then current" is a sentence the grammar cannot act on. Renewal is a new assent by the filling's holder to the version then in force, a new filling. This is the wall of terms the protection is built against. The MOR lead's "her own deal" is withdrawn." *Why no sentence*: 5.10.6 already says it: what exists for the holder is "the version the filling's proof names"; 5.10.7, "Nothing changes what a filling earned except an assent its holder signs"; 5.10.8, a later version "MAY offer again", and "a filling under it has the rights that version offers" (the scribe's reading; the trace, N1's rows to check). The lead's "a client may do it for her automatically, her dial, above the core" is the lead's, in the exchange, and is not in the text. Scenario 8.24, the wall of terms.

**5.10.7** (G10.6, changes; G10.6(b) goes). *What changed*: G10.6 comes back with (a) only; "Nothing changes what a filling earned except an assent its holder signs in their own name (5.6.1(a)), whether or not it is counted for the later version, or one made for them under a power they gave in an act of their own (5.6.1(b), 5.6.5(f))" (wave five's words, P1-42); "Nothing stops those rights but what that version's own words say". *The exchanges*: "A buyer under a replaced version" (26 November): "Version 2 is what she bought, and she has no say in the update."; "Yes, it was a bad idea. It’s the “let me drown you in terms updatable unilaterally” nightmare we already live under"; and, asked why (b) had been added at all, "Yes, but me saying something does not mean that something was necessarily a good idea". The lead's line: "this replaces "Earned rights leave the grammar" (18 November). G10.6 comes back with (a) only … A deal that ends by itself (a month's plan, a licence ending at a creator's statement) says so in its own version's words; that is not an update and (a) does not stop it." The power clause is the lead's reading on G10.6(a), answering item 18 (section 6).

**5.10.8** (N6, new; with G10.7, changes). *What it says*: what a filling gave lasts as long as its version's words say, measured on the clock that version names, from the anchoring of the purchase; the cMIP that reads the words answers whether it still applies; the verifier shows that answer, per clock, and gives none of its own; no condition triggers. *The exchange*: "Ana's month ends by the clock the agreement names" (27 November, 07:50–07:53): the conflict of 17 November, "rights stop only because a valid update says so", under which, with G10.6(a) only, "a month could never end". He: "The clock specified in the agreement and its anchoring." and "The 17th is what lead to the 18th, we”re in a different “world now. We changed how the unknown party is protected. So, valid update does not apply in the same way. There is no valid update to sign, only signed terms to respect. It’s a problem that really only impacts subscriptions models I think". *The lead's line*: "what a filling gave lasts as long as its version's words say, measured on the clock the Terms name, from the anchoring of the purchase; the cMIP that reads the words answers it, and the verifier shows the answer … Terms state their anchors (a keeper, a clock to anchor to, or both)." The line says "the clock the Terms name"; draft 26 says "the clock that version names", on the lead's reading of 13:20 (section 6). "Terms state their anchors" as a rule for all Terms is beyond his words (item 30), and is not written. From G10.7 stays "No condition triggers: these grammar rules have no answer **triggered**"; its "none that a filling's rights no longer apply" goes, the verifier now showing the cMIP's answer. Left out: which act the purchase's anchoring anchors (item 12); a version whose words state no term (item 11).

**Wave five, the readings carried** (P1's edits, and P2's in encoding 3; every row the MOR lead's, `docs/lead/notes/2026-11-27-draft-26-readings.md`, in full in section 6.14). One line for each row, under the rule it shaped first:

- **F17, C10**: "A spot a known party fills is a known party's act; she is no unknown party there; grammar case 74 is kept, renamed." *Shaped*: 5.4.3, 5.6.1(c), 5.6.2 (P1-55 to 57, with §3.2 **Unknown party**, P1-6, and 5.10.6, P1-40).
- **F9, F10, C24**: "A filling's assent gives no power; a power can be used for her only where she gave it in an act of her own. Her version's K1 and K5 cMIPs are given her version and its acts, not later versions: the grammar hands them nothing a later version made. Her own assent to a later version is her signature, counted or not." *Shaped*: 5.10.7 (P1-42), 5.10.2 (P1-39), 5.10.8 (P1-44), with 5.6.1(b) (P1-29).
- **C23 (a filling under a dropped role)**: "A spot named by the version and position the asker gives (B)." *Shaped*: 5.10.2 (P1-39).
- **C27 (a filling's term with no clock)**: "Nothing shown." *Shaped*: 5.10.8 (P1-43).

### §5.11 Legs of a sequence

*Draft 25's lines* (G11), still holding: *Lie exposed*: ""It all went through", said by one side of a leg alone; "you never asked", said by those whose own signers did; "your payment never arrived", said to a buyer who holds its confirmation; and "you agreed to be watched", said to people whose signed Terms name no such thing." *Protects*: "Both sides of every leg, who each hold the other's signed word; the buyer, who holds the confirmation of their own payment; and the people of every leg, whose reports move nowhere they did not sign for." How two halves of a leg are read together goes to the MICS with the report block ("Where three rules live", 17 November: "Agreed"); the lines stand for what people sign, which stays.

No rule of §5.11 changed: G11.1, G11.2, G11.3, G11.4(e), G11.6(a), G11.6(b), G11.7, G11.8 and G14.5(e) stay, the two-way ones on the lead's readings (section 6), with sentences on how a report is read cut for the MICS. The baker's lines of draft 25 (G14) go to Envelopes with its rules; 5.11.9's contest stays ("Where three rules live": "Agreed for the first, not sure I get the second, expand on third too").

**Wave five, the readings carried** (P1's edits, and P2's in encoding 3; every row the MOR lead's, `docs/lead/notes/2026-11-27-draft-26-readings.md`, in full in section 6.14). One line for each row, under the rule it shaped first:

- **C32 (a contest by one who is not the holder)**: "Shown as a contest, not shown as a baker's." *Shaped*: 5.11.9 (P1-46).

### §5.12 Forks

*Draft 25's lines* (G12), still holding: *Lie exposed*: ""This one is the real one", said of one tip to whoever is shown only that tip, or shown it as settled." *Protects*: "Whoever relied on a tip without causing the fork; everyone in the last shared version, which goes on while the fork stands; and the reader, who is shown every tip, and none of them as resolved." *Widened* (the scribe's): the fork now covers any two acts competing to change the same state, so the same lines hold for a coming into force against a counter signature: "we are in force" and "my signature was taken back in time" are both exposed where nothing orders them.

**5.12.1** (N8, new). *What it says*: "Where two acts compete to change the same state, with no order shown between them, these grammar rules know one thing: the fork"; there may be a race inside it, or it may be resolved among all involved, and the grammar reads both alike; neither act takes effect until a new act resolves it; "How forks are treated in different cases is not these grammar rules'". *The exchange*: "A fork is a fork" (27 November, 07:01–07:08). The MOR lead set the race rule of 17 and 18 November beside the fork of 26 November and leaned to split them. He: "MOR and coffee…"; "Two valid updates? Sort of… and that”s where I struggled. The case of one buyer being on v2 while v3 exists is not a fork. Or are we talking about something else?"; "So, for you a race and a fork are two things… but from a MOR point of view they are not. (Slow start…)"; "Not that it can, from core pov it has to. If they have to be treated differently under different scenarios that is another question."; and "A fork is a fork. When a fork exists there could be a race between a white party and a black party. Or, the fork could be resolved amicably by all emvolved". *The lead's line*: "the core knows one thing, the fork … "Race" is not a term of the core: it names what may happen inside a fork, an adversarial one, as against one resolved amicably by all involved." Why one word (the scribe's reading of the exchange): a verifier cannot tell from bytes whether two competing acts are a conflict or an accident; it can tell only that nothing orders them. "Fork" is also the answer word (the brief's item 9, answered so by the outline, section 10, row 1).

**5.12.2** (G12.1, changes). *What changed*: a fork is "two acts competing to change the same state, with no order shown between them": two updates of one act of a line, a coming into force against a counter signature, "or any other"; the delegation clause and "those whose assent G4.1 asks for" go. *The exchanges*: "A fork is a fork" (27 November): "Not that it can, from core pov it has to."; "Delegation is a leftover" (18 November); "Who signs an update by default" (26 November). The test for "the same state" is left out (item 8).

**5.12.4** (G12.3, changes). *What changed*: a resolving update of Terms is signed by default by the known parties; one whose `resolves` names no fork in its own line is answered **not shown**, "nothing to resolve"; `resolves` names the tips "by the list of the tips' ids"; the form is given for a fork of a line only. *The exchanges*: "A resolve with nothing to resolve: "not shown", with the reason" (26 November, 15:45–15:48). He: "Agreed. It’s a different reason for a “sorry can’t do” and it’s always good to state why". The lead's line: ""Unchecked" is kept for a reader that lacks something. Every refusal states its own reason." And "Who signs an update by default" (26 November). The list of ids is the lead's reading of 09:47, row 6 (section 6). How a fork not of a line is resolved in bytes is left out (item 7).

**Wave five, the readings carried** (P1's edits, and P2's in encoding 3; every row the MOR lead's, `docs/lead/notes/2026-11-27-draft-26-readings.md`, in full in section 6.14). One line for each row, under the rule it shaped first:

- **F5, the self-fork after an update is in force**: "A fork of a line made after an update is in force does not reach back: the update stays in force, and the fork is that signer's, to resolve in her line. The text says so in one sentence (5.12.3's last, made a rule)." *Shaped*: 5.12.3 (P1-47).
- **C39 (a line resolve's word)**: "Shown." *Shaped*: 5.12.4 (P1-48).

### §5.13 Knots: what an act names, and "shown after"

*Draft 25's lines* (G13), still holding, one word read anew: *Lie exposed*: ""You paid the wrong one", said to a payer after the agreement changed behind them; and "nothing came after", said on an anchoring the agreement does not name." *Protects*: "Whoever acts on a version they read and named: the payer above all." *Widened* (the scribe's): every fingerprint in any field of any act is now a knot, so "nothing came after" is also exposed by an act of another layer that names it.

**5.13.1** (G13.1, two ways in the brief; changes on the lead's reading). *What changed*: an act is read against what it names "in any field that holds a fingerprint … the body's field included"; "Each such naming is a knot (§3.4): any fingerprint in any field of any act the verifier holds, whatever layer either act belongs to; a verifier reads the fields of every layer of the core (§2). A verifier reads knots in fields, never in an act's content." The list of fields now names "the layer below's `prev` and `refs`" (wave five, P1-49 and P1-50, the lead's readings F6/C26 and C25). *The exchange*: "Knots are knots" (27 November), under 5.13.3. The lead's reading on G13.1 (section 6).

**5.13.3** (N7, new). *What it says*: "Act B is shown after act A when the verifier holds acts, each naming the one before by fingerprint, that lead from B back to A, or a keeper's record or an anchoring that places them so"; the one reading, for every rule that asks whether one act came after another; "It proves that B came after A, and nothing more: never that an act came before one that does not name it, and never that an act does not exist"; a missing act named in a field is **unchecked**, "never a yes, and never a no". *The exchanges*: "Knots are knots: one reading, "shown after"" (27 November, 07:38–07:49). The MOR lead leaned that Agreements read for order only its own lines, keepers and anchoring. He: "I struggle with the first one because knots are knots… in ordering any knot that can be leveraged should be leveraged. We are trying to identify order in one layer, but it is very likely that knots on other layers tied to the IDs of the agreement can be useful, no?"; "Missing act is always “I don’t know” my brain encounters such moments fairly often a question arrives, I consider it, and decide that I miss the data needed to reply."; "Every knot counts… counts for who? And how does it count? What are we defining here?"; "My question I think was… are we defining how knots should be leveraged? Are we simply ensuring there are enough knots going around for people to find ways of leveraging them?"; and last: "Knots a cross acts by nature, and possibly cross layer. An agreement pointing to another agreement is a knot, the agreement pointing to the IDs is another, the agreement pointing to the envelope is another, the envelope to the IDs is others and so on…" The MOR lead answered his question "both: the core ensures the knots exist, defines the one reading ("shown after", since a verdict must come out the same for every verifier), uses it for its own three questions (late, the pin, a signature in a line), and leaves every other use to cMIPs and clients." The one-line reading is the lead's, not his words (item 28, section 6). And "A missing act stays unchecked: no door around it" (26 November, 18:34–18:57). He: "Wait… missing “block” problem let’s zoom out"; "Dunno, I feel it should stay unchecked. Ikm trying to understand why the feeling is this strong"; "I think yes. I don’t see the need to open a door around it. But feel a risk"; "No, I meant a risk of opening a door". The lead's answer there: "a missing act must never become a no, as it never becomes a yes; a door would let whoever controls what a reader holds control the verdict, and would make verdicts flip as acts arrive". The lead's line: "The stall is the forced deadlock: only someone the update condition accepts can make it, the agreement resolves it as a fork, and a keeper prevents it in advance" (item 32, section 6).

*Why knots matter in Agreements* (the importance §0 sends to "where this text treats relying on acts"; the scribe's reading of the exchanges above). Where the Terms name no keeper, knots are the only order the agreement has: the pin of a first version (5.4.1), of an update (5.5.1(b)), whether an update is late (5.5.4(a)), whether a counter signature came in time (5.4.5), whether an act under a power came before a replacement (5.6.4). Where the Terms name keepers, knots expose a keeper that lies (5.8.4). An act that names what it relied on (5.13.2) makes a knot that every verifier can read; one that does not leaves its order to others. "Knots making is core core business" (16:16); "The core ensures knots exist" (the lead's line, 07:49) is not written as a rule (the trace, M7's rows to check, 10).

**5.13.4** (G13.3, changes). *What changed*: order from naming, "with no keeper and no qualifier"; before or after Terms came into force, from naming, "where the Terms name no keeper", against the gate and the pin; per keeper, at "the first position at which, counting only the acts the keepers show at or before it, they pass the gate and the pin". *The exchanges*: "Knots are knots" (27 November): "Knots a cross acts by nature, and possibly cross layer."; "The gate and the pin" (26 November). The keepers' scope is the lead's reading of 10:07 (section 6).

**5.13.5** (G13.4, changes). *What changed*: draft 25's "Only where the agreement names none does each reader accept the anchoring it chooses" goes; a reader's own anchoring, "whether or not the agreement names any, gives an answer only as that reader's, named so, given beside the Terms' answer and never in its place: it never gives the Terms' own answer, never puts Terms in force, and never makes an update late". *The exchanges*: "A condition in time with no anchor is refused" (26 November): "Yes, condition is missing, it should be refused"; "Whose place counts" (26 November): "I think you are right. It is worth turning it into a principle. Protect the unknown party". The lead's reading on G1.4, reaching it (section 6); "given beside the Terms' answer and never in its place", the lead's reading C31/F8 (section 6.14).

**Wave five, the readings carried** (P1's edits, and P2's in encoding 3; every row the MOR lead's, `docs/lead/notes/2026-11-27-draft-26-readings.md`, in full in section 6.14). One line for each row, under the rule it shaped first:

- **F6, C26 (`refs` and `prev` as knots)**: "Knots. Encoding 3's E3 is wrong and follows draft 26." *Shaped*: 5.13.1 (P1-49); encoding 3, E3 (P2).
- **C25 (which layers' fields)**: "Every layer of the core; a verifier decodes the core's six." *Shaped*: 5.13.1 (P1-50).
- **C31, F8 (the reader's own anchoring or keeper)**: "Given beside the Terms' answer, labelled the reader's, never in its place." *Shaped*: 5.13.5 (P1-51).
