### N10: relying, by the client, when it signs (client conformance)

*Status*: not a rule of draft 26. The lead's reading of 09:16: "N10 (the client's reliance) is client conformance in the companion, not a rule." Its place among the client duties is listed for Development (M10). *What it is*: when a party signs an agreement, its client also signs a witness act (Identity draft 16, rule 10) relying on the words' Text act, so that if the words' signer later leaves them out by a change of key, rule 34 keeps them visible, as disputed. *The exchange*: "Signing an agreement relies on its words" (26 November, 18:30–18:37). The MOR lead leaned that Terms naming their words count as relying on them, a new standing in Identity. He: "Clients can easily fix this by automatically rely on the text when an agreement is signed." and (18:37): "Just to finish… rely is the solution to all acts someone has to rely on." *The lead's lines*: "nothing is added to Identity or Agreements. When a party signs an agreement, its client also signs a witness act relying on the words' Text act (client conformance) … The MOR lead's lean is not taken"; and "whoever needs another's act to keep standing relies on it (a witness act, signed by their client). Relying is how any act someone depends on survives its signer's key change." Left out: whether her client relies on the other parties' assents too (item 21; the settled note's F4); that private words' key is delivered to each party first (item 22; F10). The one client duty draft 26 itself carries is 5.12.9.

## 6. The readings marked as the MOR lead's

**In plain words.** In some places the ledger could be read two ways, or said less than a rule needs. There the MOR lead chose one reading, and draft 26 is written on it. Each such reading is set out here: the lead's words, the rule it shaped, and what the other reading would have put in the text. None of them is his decision; each can be revisited with him.

None of these readings is in the ledger on main: each was given by the MOR lead to the writing sessions on 27 November and is quoted as the trace has it. Where a time is given, it is the lead's; the rulings on the brief's items and on the held rules came with the outline's and the sessions' assignments, and no time is recorded for them. Where a reading still leaves a choice, it is listed, not made: "Where a reading above meets a rule and still leaves a choice, list it; do not choose." (the lead).

Each entry gives, after the reading, *Shaped*: the rules written on it; and *The other reading*: what the brief's row, or the trace's row to check that asked it, gave as the other way; and, where it is plain, what a verifier would answer on it (the scribe's reading).

### 6.1 The rulings on the brief's heaviest items

**Item 1: the pin for a first version.** The lead: "a first version still waits for the pin. "In force once pinned" (18 November) carries over; the 16:19 line was about who signs, not when it counts." *Shaped*: 5.4.1 (the pin), 5.4.5 (until the completion), 5.6.6(c); §3.3, Pin and In force. *The other reading* (the brief, G4.1 (i)): the first version comes into force by its assents alone, as the 16:19 line has it ("The first version comes into force by the assents of its named parties") and the settled note's M19 ("Unchanged"). On it, a verifier answers **in force** for a first version as soon as the assents its `in_force` rule asks for are held; on the ruling, it answers **not shown**, no order being shown, until the keepers it names show those assents or each party whose assent counted has acted again, shown after them. Where the pin closes the counter signature's window turned on this too (the brief, G4.10).

**Item 4: a keeper's record, and Identity's rule 34.** The lead: "a keeper's record for Identity's rule 34 waits for draft 26 to exist; write draft 26's keeper as decided, and list rule 34 under "for Identity"." *Shaped*: 5.8.1 (the keeper as decided: the pointer, the chaining only); §2 names Identity's rule 34 and not its keeper's record; the 09:16 reading below then defined a keeper's record for draft 26's own use. *The other reading* (the brief, item 4): Identity draft 16 gives an act standing where "a named keeper recorded it" and says "Keepers MUST (see Agreements)", reading draft 24's keeper record; draft 26 could have defined a keeper's record as Identity reads it. Not chosen. Until Identity is next written, its rule 34 names a record draft 26 does not give (M10's "for Identity").

**Item 28: "shown after".** The lead: ""shown after" stays the lead's reading, marked so in the companion." *The reading*, as the lead's line of "Knots are knots" (27 November) has it and draft 26 writes it (§3.4, 5.13.3): "act B is shown after act A when the verifier holds a chain of acts, each naming the one before by fingerprint, from B back to A, or a keeper's record or an anchoring that places them. It proves after, never before, and never absence". *Against his last words*: his last words in the exchange are "Knots a cross acts by nature, and possibly cross layer. An agreement pointing to another agreement is a knot, the agreement pointing to the IDs is another, the agreement pointing to the envelope is another, the envelope to the IDs is others and so on…", said after his question "My question I think was… are we defining how knots should be leveraged? Are we simply ensuring there are enough knots going around for people to find ways of leveraging them?", which the MOR lead answered "both". His words say which namings are knots, and before that "in ordering any knot that can be leveraged should be leveraged"; they do not give the one-line reading. *Shaped*: every rule that asks whether one act came after another: the pin (5.4.1, 5.5.1(b)), a counter signature (5.4.5), a late update (5.5.4), an act under a power (5.6.4), a signer's line (§5.7), and 5.13.3 to 5.13.5. *The other reading* (the scribe's, from the brief's item 28): his words define the knots and leave how they are leveraged open; a verifier would then have no single test of "after", and two honest verifiers could read the same knots differently (whether a keeper's record or an anchoring places two acts; whether a chain through another layer counts). For example: on the reading, an update L is **late**, as shown, only where the verifier holds a chain of namings in fields from L back to the acts that put the replacing version through, or the keepers' records place it so; without the one reading, whether a mention of that version in L's words, or a chain through an act of another layer, shows L after would be each verifier's to judge, and one could answer **late** where another answers **fork**. The lead's "since a verdict must come out the same for every verifier" is the reason given for fixing it.

**The other unsaid items.** The lead: "write draft 26 where the ledger or a note answers them; where only a new choice would, leave the rule out and list it." *Shaped*: what draft 26 leaves out, item by item (the outline, section 7; each session's "left out"). The items a note answers (19, 21, 22, 23, 25, 26) are written on the note, and the note is the lead's reading.

### 6.2 Lines that say more than his words

These are the ledger's own "Arrived at" lines where the brief found they go beyond what he said (its items 29 to 33). Draft 26 follows each as far as his words reach; where a line goes further, the further part is the lead's reading.

**Item 31: the wording of the gate and the pin.** "The gate and the pin" (26 November): the wording of G4.8 and G4.7 ("an update replaces only if (a) the update condition answers valid and (b) its order after the last replacement is shown, per the place or by the knots"; of two updates of one version, "the later is late where (b) orders them, and where nothing orders them they are a fork") is "the MOR lead's wording for draft 26". His last words confirm the forced deadlock ("Ok, that”s what I was trying to remember we forced the deadlock and push them to resolve it"), not the wording. *Shaped*: 5.5.1, 5.5.4, §3.3 Gate and Pin. "Its order after the last replacement" read in two parts (shown after the acts that put the named version in force; every rival that passes the gate shown after it) is M4's, confirmed by the lead's reading of 09:47 below. *The other reading*: the same deadlock with other words; the brief names no rival wording.

**Item 32: the stall.** "A missing act stays unchecked" (26 November): "only someone the update condition accepts can make it" and "a keeper prevents it in advance" are the lead's; his words are "I think yes. I don’t see the need to open a door around it. But feel a risk" and "No, I meant a risk of opening a door". *Shaped*: nothing written as a rule; 5.13.3's "No grammar rule goes around it" is his. *The other reading*: none of a verifier's answers turns on it: a missing act named in a field is **unchecked** either way. Who can cause a stall, and how a keeper prevents it, are explanation (M10's stated costs).

**Item 33: the default signers.** "Who signs an update by default: the known parties" (26 November), marked "the MOR lead's reading" in the ledger: his words, "It’s the protect the unknown party expressed the old way.", name no default signers; the known parties come from his earlier "only the known parties can" (15:52). *Shaped*: 5.5.5(a), 5.5.7(e), 5.6.5(a), 5.6.6(b), 5.12.4, §3.2 Known party. *The other reading*: his 15:52 words were about who has a say in updates; draft 25's default (every signer of the original, fillers included) could have stood beside them, the protection then being a veto. On it, an update signed by the known parties alone is **not shown** as a replacement while any filler's assent is lacking; on the reading, it is **valid**, by default.

Also beyond his words, and not written (left out): item 29 (the line's "her rights, her spot, her place in the agreement" and "close the offer, or dissolve it among themselves", his words ending "No?"); item 30 ("Terms state their anchors (a keeper, a clock to anchor to, or both)", beyond his "valid update does not apply in the same way").

### 6.3 The held rules

The brief read fifteen rules two ways. The lead read each one way; draft 26 is written on those readings. Of the readings on G11.3, G11.4(e), G11.5, G11.6(b), G11.6(c) and G14.5(e), each says which text a sentence sits in (draft 26, the MICS, Envelopes): no answer of draft 26's verifier turns on it, though a verifier of the MICS's report block then answers what draft 25 had answered here (the scribe's reading).

**G1.4, reaching G4.6, G4.7(b), G7.4, G10.5 and G13.4.** The lead: "the clause "per the anchoring the reader accepts" goes; a reader's own anchoring never gives the Terms' answer". *Shaped*: 5.1.4, 5.5.3, 5.5.4(b), 5.8.5, 5.10.5, 5.13.5. *The other reading* (the brief, G1.4 (i)): the clause stays, the answer being already named as the reader's, as "Whose place counts" allows. On it, where the Terms name no keeper, a reader that accepts an anchoring may give which Terms are in force, or that an update is late, per that anchoring, labelled; on the reading, a reader's anchoring "never puts Terms in force, and never makes an update late", and the Terms' own answer is only per keeper, per clock or as shown.

**G3.1, with the reading of 09:27.** See 6.5.

**G4.1.** Item 1, 6.1.

**G4.8(g).** The lead: "G4.8(g): the clause restating the default goes to the companion ("Only rules in the hashed text")". *Shaped*: nothing in the text; its history comes to section 11. *The other reading* (the brief): it changes and stays, read as the default said again. No answer of a verifier turns on it: the default is in 5.5.5(a).

**G4.9 and G4.11(i), reaching G5.7(a) and K4.** The lead: "as "What a dissolution is" (18 November) has them; the update condition decides, as its cMIP dictates; a version that "cannot be updated" is one whose update condition answers not valid to every update". *Shaped*: 5.5.6, 5.5.7(i), 5.6.6(a), K4. *The other reading* (the brief, G4.9, as the first brief found it): an agreement "can always end" by an update stating its dissolution, against "a verifier MUST NOT answer that Terms or an agreement have ended"; and "can never be updated" against a dissolution's "Cannot be updated.". On the other reading, a verifier could answer that an agreement has ended once a version stating its dissolution is in force. On the reading, a dissolution is an update like any other and the verifier never answers "ended"; what it shows of an agreement the known parties dissolved while a filling still binds is left out (item 13).

**G5.6(f) and G10.6(a).** The lead: "G5.6(f) and G10.6(a) under a power: stay; an assent made for a party under a power she gave is her signing ("A power given binds its giver"; settled note, M22)." *Shaped*: 5.6.5(f), 5.6.1(b), 5.10.7 (the power clause), 5.6.6(b). Answers item 18. *The other reading* (the brief, G10.6(a) (i)): "in their own name" only, as the 26 November line has it ("No later version changes the rights of a filling already earned except by an assent its holder signs in their own name"). On it, an assent made for the holder of a filling by an agent under a power she gave would not change what the filling earned; on the reading, it does, and 5.10.7 names that power.

**G6.1 and G6.2, with E23.** The lead: "G6.1, G6.2, and E23 with them: stay in Agreements; `previous` is the agreement's thread, per signer, which "Two threads, two owners" gives to Agreements; the life-long line (`prev`) is the layer below's." *Shaped*: 5.7.1, 5.7.2; §3.3 Line; §2 (Identity, Envelopes); §4.3, an author's first act. *The other reading* (the brief): the signer's line is the lower layer's, and G6 moves. On it, Agreements would not itself answer **fork** for two acts of one signer within one agreement that name the same `previous`; the layer below would.

**G11.3 and K6.** The lead: "G11.3, K6: stay, as what K6 asks of a leg report (what people sign); the sentences on how a report is read go to the MICS with the report block ("Where three rules live")." *Shaped*: 5.11.3; K6's row (§6); the sentences cut from G11.2, G11.3, G11.7 and K6's row, listed for the MICS. *The other reading* (the brief): G11.3 leaves whole with the report block.

**G11.4(e).** The lead: "G11.4(e): stays; it answers from Terms what calls exist." *Shaped*: 5.11.4. *The other reading*: it goes with the reading of reports.

**G11.5 and G11.6(c).** The lead: "G11.5, G11.6(c): move to the MICS with the report block (they speak of every layer)." *Shaped*: not in draft 26. *The other reading*: they stay (the brief: one sentence of G11.6(c) is the block's, one is how people agree to a further path).

**G11.6(b).** The lead: "G11.6(b): stays; who may see is the data socket's cMIP ("Seeing and being told")." *Shaped*: 5.11.6. *The other reading* (the brief): it changes, being narrower than the guarantee it gives.

**G13.1.** The lead: "G13.1: changes to the one reading: any fingerprint in any field, the body's included, is a knot; "shown after" as "Knots are knots" has it." *Shaped*: 5.13.1, §3.4 Knot, 5.5.4(a). *The other reading* (the brief, G13.1 (ii)): it stays as draft 25 has it, a list of fields an act is read against, with "shown after" written elsewhere. On it, the fingerprint in `body` (the words' Text act) and fingerprints in fields draft 25 did not list would not be knots for order.

**G14.5(e).** The lead: "G14.5(e): stays in Agreements as the contest (what people sign), worded on the holder read from the envelope's chain, the baker's rule itself being Envelopes'." *Shaped*: 5.11.9. *The other reading*: it moves to Envelopes with the baker's rule.

### 6.4 The reading of 09:16, on M2's rows

1. "the thirteen MICS rules sit in the companion, under "for the MICS", with draft 25's text; not in a §8 of the hashed text. "§8" was the lead's slip; §2 should name the companion instead." *Shaped*: §2, the MICS line. *The other reading* (M2's row 1): a §8 inside draft 26 holding them. No verifier answer turns on it.
2. ""a keeper's record" is defined by draft 26 for its own use (what a keeper lodges in its chain for an act: the act's id and its position); Identity's rule 34 keeps pointing at draft 24's until Identity is touched; list it under "for Identity"." *Shaped*: §3.4, Keeper's record; 5.8.1. *The other reading* (M2's row 5): "a keeper's record" left undefined, or defined as Identity draft 16 reads it; the two "may be one thing or two".
3. ""Uploader" is his word, wider than Envelopes draft 12's; draft 26 uses it as he said it and lists it for Envelopes." *Shaped*: §3.5, Lodge. *The other reading* (M2's row 6): Envelopes' narrower word (a bake's signer).
4. "N10 (the client's reliance) is client conformance in the companion, not a rule." *Shaped*: N10 is not in draft 26 (section 5, N10); §3.6 Client says one client duty is written, in 5.12.9. *The other reading* (the brief, item 17): N10 written in Agreements beside 5.12.9. No verifier answer turns on it: no verifier can check a client duty (5.12.9).

### 6.5 The reading of 09:27, on M3's questions

1. "Author: a signer the Terms name, never an agreement, as draft 25's G3.1 and E13 have it; nothing in this stint changes it. M2's Author definition and 5.3.1 narrow to "named as a signer"; "who signs them" becomes "who sign them". The lead's reading of "named party"; mark it so in the trace." *Shaped*: §3.2 Author; 5.3.1; §4.2 `authors`; the one-fault list, case 5. *The other readings* (M3's row 2; the brief, G3.1): an author named as an agreement (M2's first wording), or by role (draft 25's G3.1, (i)). On the reading, Terms whose `authors` name a party named by role or as an agreement are **invalid**.
2. "The set of acts: a value form for fields that name several acts with no order between them: the grammar's `resolves`, and any parameter of a cMIP that asks for acts. Whether another grammar field takes it is an open parameter (§7). The lead's reading." *Shaped*: §4.1, §7. Narrowed at 09:47 (row 6, below): `resolves` is no longer among them.
3. "The act `body` names, held and not a Text act: the Terms are invalid, as an act missing what it needs ("Text comes first"); the lead's reading, list it." *Shaped*: 5.2.2; the one-fault list, case 2. *The other reading* (M3's left-out item: "No source says"): **unchecked**, or not read at all. On the reading, such Terms are **invalid**.
4. "Whether the Terms act's signer must be among its authors: left out, for Nobody, allegedly; carry it to the end of the stage." *Shaped*: nothing; carried for him.

### 6.6 The reading of 09:47, on M4's rows

1. "Row 6: `resolves` carries the list of the tips' ids, as draft 25 has it; a resolving update whose tips a verifier cannot read resolves nothing. The set form is for fields where a name for a set is enough (a cMIP's parameter, a body's evidence); the lead's line of 27 November putting `resolves` among them is withdrawn. §4.1 and §4.3 accordingly; `continues` is one of the ids in `resolves`." *Shaped*: §4.1, §4.2, §4.3 (`resolves`, `continues`), 5.12.4. *The other reading* (M4's row 6): `resolves` holds a set's value; then "from a set's value alone neither can be checked, and the tips are no longer knots of the resolving update". The lead's own reading also holds a sentence draft 26 does not write, listed by M7: "a resolving update whose tips a verifier cannot read resolves nothing". With `resolves` back to the list it seems to speak of the withdrawn set form; read of a tip named and not held, it would stand against "Missing act is always “I don’t know”".
2. "Row 2: any act of a needed signer within the agreement, shown after the completion, pins the Terms; a counter signature so shown is late and cancels nothing ("In force once pinned": "no signature taken back after it"); one nothing orders is a fork. 5.4.1's "not an act that pins" goes." *Shaped*: 5.4.1, 5.4.5. *The other reading* (M4's row 2): a counter signature is not an act that pins. On it, a counter signature shown after the completing assents would neither pin nor cancel; on the reading, it pins and cancels nothing.
3. "Row 3: an update with roles carries `in_force` for who fills a role, in every version; only its answer on coming into force is unread for updates (settled note, M11, Q7 narrowed). Row 1: agreed, not written." *Shaped*: 5.4.2; 5.2.3; §4.2 `in_force`; K2. Row 1: draft 25's clause "Terms that name no party as a signer, and carry no `in_force`, are in force at once" is not written, having no case once every author is a signer. *The other reading* (M4's row 3): an update's `in_force` wholly unread (Q7 unnarrowed), so that an update with roles carries a field nobody reads. Still open (M6's row 1): whether K2 or K5 says who fills a role; 5.10.2 reads only the K5 cMIP.
4. "Late update, one test: a version is late when it is shown after the acts that put the version it should have named through the gate and the pin. §3.3 and 5.5.4 both on it. §3.3's Pin: the two parts of 5.5.1(b)." *Shaped*: §3.3 Late update and Pin; 5.5.4. *The other reading* (M4's "for … words", 6): §3.3's earlier "shown after the version that replaced it", the Terms act itself. On it, L would be late once shown after F's bytes, even before F passed the gate and the pin; on the reading, only once shown after the acts that put F through both.
5. "The agreement named as a party that never signed: it is a known party and among the default signers; how it signs is its own rule's (G1), and an update it does not sign is not valid by default. The lead's reading; list it." *Shaped*: 5.5.5(a). *The other reading* (M4's left-out item, from M2's blind reader): such a party is not among the default signers, as it is not counted for a first version (5.4.4). On it, an update the other known parties sign is **valid** by default; on the reading, it is **not shown** as a replacement until that agreement signs.
6. The found item: "The found item on a resolved fork: as the resolver decides; the 18 November "withdrawal stands" and the 27 November "neither takes effect" agree (not in force, she is not bound); companion." Section 5, under 5.4.5. Nothing is written in the text for it.

### 6.7 The reading of 10:07, on M5's rows

1. "Row 2: where the Terms name keepers, only the keepers pin ("The place is a pointer", 17 November: "from it and from nothing else"); knots do not pin in their place, and an act the keepers do not yet show is not yet in force. Knots still expose a keeper: a keeper's record that contradicts a knot the verifier holds (B before A, while A names B) gives "not shown, the keeper contradicts the acts", the record kept as evidence; the keeper's lie is signed. 5.5.4(c) reads so: a late update is shown late by knots against a keeper only as that keeper's contradiction. The lead's reading. Row 3 follows: the window closes at the completion as the keepers show it, or by knots where none is named." *Shaped*: 5.4.1, 5.5.1(b), §3.3 Pin, 5.5.4(a) and (c), 5.8.4, 5.13.3, 5.13.4; for row 3, 5.4.5. *The other reading* (M5's row 2): knots pin too where keepers are named ("Knots are knots": "in ordering any knot that can be leveraged should be leveraged"). On it, Terms whose needed signers have acted after the completion would be in force by knots even where the keepers named do not yet show them; on the reading, they are not yet in force. For row 3 (M5's row 3): the window closes at the pin, as draft 25 and M4 had it; on the reading, at the completion.
2. "Row 6: "naming neither" is the lead's loose phrase; the rule: Terms that do not supply what a cMIP they name declares it needs (a clock, a keeper) are invalid. A keeper is not a clock. 5.8.6 so." *Shaped*: 5.8.6, 5.5.7(a) and (c), §6's opening. *The other reading* (M5's row 6): the ledger's line read literally ("the Terms naming neither"). On it, Terms that name a keeper and no clock are not invalid even where the cMIP needs a clock; on the reading, they are **invalid**.
3. "Row 5: 5.7.3 to the companion; E23's test to encoding 3. The two Development items: list them under "for Development"; the agent's own binding (the blind reader's gap): list it for Nobody, allegedly, with the signer-among-authors question." *Shaped*: 5.7.3's two lines are in section 5 (§5.7); the rest is M10's and encoding 3's. No verifier answer turns on it.

### 6.8 The reading of 13:20, on M6's question

1. "a filling's clock and keepers are its own version's, never the Terms in force; "There is no valid update for her anymore. Only a valid agreement" reaches the clock as it reaches everything else. 5.4.3 and 5.10.8 stand; your §5.10 line reads "its own version". §3.3's Apply on it. 5.10.2 (a filling read from its own version once its role is dropped) is the same rule said for the role; keep it, marked the lead's reading." *Shaped*: §3.3 Apply ("never that of the Terms in force"); 5.4.3, 5.5.7(f), 5.10.6, 5.10.8; 5.10.2's last sentence. *The other reading* (M6's row 4; the lead's line of "Ana's month": "measured on the clock the Terms name"; the outline): the clock of the Terms in force. On it, where a later version names another clock, her month is counted on the new clock; on the reading, on her own version's. For 5.10.2, the other reading is draft 25's: the role and its cMIP read from the Terms in force, so that a filling under a role a later version drops has nothing to read it by.
2. "The holder of the baker's rights for a contest: read at the act the Terms cite ("The baker": "The holder at the time"); the lead's reading, list it." *Shaped*: 5.11.9. *Cited* (the lead's reading of 15:34, item 3, 6.12 below): the ledger's heading is "The holder at the act cited" (17 November); the 13:20 words are kept as given. *The other reading* (M6's left-out item): at the contest. Still unclear (M7's row 9): the act cited is in the contested Terms' `envelope_at`, which names an act of their envelope, not the baker's.
3. "Carry the two for Nobody, allegedly, to the end of the stage." The two: whether the Terms act's signer must be among its `authors`; whether an agent who signs for another is bound by that act.

### 6.9 The settled note's rows draft 26 is written on

Every row of the settled note is the lead's reading. Those draft 26 rests on, with the rule each shaped: **M5** (the answer words; "Rejected" is not one): §3.6, 5.5.4(c). **M11, Q7** (an update's `in_force`, narrowed at 09:47): 5.4.2, 5.2.3. **M12**, against the ledger's 17:49 line, not used: 5.8.3 (item 25). **M15** (a filling is any filling of a role, whenever made; an assent to a replaced version shown, its version named): §3.2 Unknown party, 5.4.3. **M20** (a reader's chosen keeper or anchoring only labelled as the reader's): 5.5.3, 5.13.5. **M21, F12, F13** (the set of acts: the empty set, no knot by itself, no order, acts only): §3.1, §4.1, §7. **M22** (an assent under a power she gave is her signing): 5.10.7, 5.6.5(f). **M24** (the MICS is rewritten after draft 26): §2. **M28** (words that renew on the terms then current, bought knowingly): not written (item 14). Item 14 is since closed by ""Renewed on the terms then current" cannot be built" (27 November, 16:46–16:48), whose line says the grammar cannot act on such words and withdraws the MOR lead's "her own deal"; where M28 says a buyer who signs them "buys that knowingly", the ledger's line wins (section 6.13). **Q22** (the warning is the introduction's): §0. **Q24, Q25** (the verifier need not hold the words; naming the Text act asks nothing more): 5.2.2, K1. **Q26** (a cMIP declares its needs; for Development): 5.8.6, §7. **F16** (G10.6(a) a guarantee no module may break): section 3.

### 6.10 The reading of 14:33, on M7's question and rows

*Added by M10, as the others are written: the lead's words, the rules each shaped, and the other reading.*

1. "Item 9: a known party who assents to both rival updates forks her own line (5.7.2), and in a fork neither tip takes effect ("A fork is a fork", 27 November; draft 25's G12.2, both tips stop). Neither assent counts for either gate until her line is resolved, by a resolving act in her line as §5.12 says. The lead's reading, marked so; it keeps one signer from putting two rivals through the gate." *Shaped*: 5.5.5(a), 5.7.2 (M10's edit (a)); scenarios 8.4 and 8.7. *The other reading* (M7's rows to check, 4, the question the reading answers: "5.12.3 spares an assent to a tip of Terms, but not an assent that is also a tip of its signer's line. Whether such an assent still counts for whether the Terms tip was signed is not said"): 5.12.3's "one thing an assent to a tip still counts for" reaches it, each assent counting for whether its Terms tip was signed. The scribe's lean on it, not taken, is not recorded in the trace in its own words; read so, a verifier would answer **fork** of Terms where one known party's double assent puts two rivals through the gate, V1 staying in force; on the reading, neither rival passes the gate on her assent, and the fork is in her line alone (scenario 8.7).
2. "Edit (f): the holder is read at the act the contest cites: a contest names the original envelope and the act of its chain it relies on, and the holder is read there, not from the contested Terms. The lead's reading." *Shaped*: 5.11.9 (M10's edit (b)). "The original envelope" is written there "its own envelope, whose baker's rights its signer holds", since draft 26's §3.3 gives "the original" to an update. Since rewritten by M11's edit (i), on the lead's reading of 15:34 (6.12, item 3): "a contest names an envelope, whose baker's rights its signer holds, and the act of that envelope's chain it relies on (5.13.2), and the holder is read at that act of the envelope the contest names". *The other reading* (M7's edit (f), and M7's rows to check, 9): the holder read at the act the contested Terms cite (their `envelope_at`), which names an act of their envelope, not the baker's.
3. "M7's rows: 5.5.4(c) reads "nothing built on L counts: a late update is no tip and gives no previous" (own words, no pointer); the colon in 5.5.1(b) goes so the two parts read for every Terms; 5.11.6 and 5.11.7 each their heading; 5.10.8 adds "where the version names no clock, no term in time can be read from it (5.8.6)"; the one-fault list under its own heading, §4.4. 5.12.4 and §7's sixth value: keep, marked added by M7, to check." *Shaped*: 5.5.4(c), 5.5.1(b), 5.11.6, 5.10.8, §4.4 (M10's edits (c) to (g)); 5.12.4's added clause and §7's "How far a verifier follows" stand, still marked added by M7, to check, in the trace. *The other reading*: for 5.5.1(b), the pin's two parts read only where keepers are named (M7's "for earlier words", 2): for Terms naming no keeper, an update would then be pinned with no order shown against its rivals; on the reading, every update's pin has both parts. The other rows change no answer of a verifier: they are words and headings (the scribe's reading).

### 6.11 The reading of 14:59, on M8 and M9

*Added by M11, as the others are written: the lead's words, the rules each shaped, and the other reading.*

1. "in 5.13.3, "an anchoring" is one to the chain the Terms name as their clock, and "a keeper's record" is one of the keepers the Terms name; any other anchoring or keeper is evidence a reader may show, labelled as the reader's, and never pins, never makes late ("Whose place counts", 26 November; "The place is a pointer", 17 November). Knots between acts (a fingerprint of one act in a field of another) count wherever the verifier holds them. M11 writes the qualifier. The lead's reading." *Shaped*: 5.13.3 and §3.4 **Shown after** (M11's edit (a)). *The other reading* (M9's "for the text", 3): "shown after" as M7 wrote it, "a keeper's record or an anchoring that places them so", unqualified; read alone, any anchoring a reader accepts would show "after" for the pin, against 5.5.3 and 5.13.5. On the reading, only a keeper the Terms name and the clock they name place two acts for the Terms' own answer.
2. "M8's open byte choices: pick none; list them in encoding 3's "Open" for the codemeister, who proposes, and for Nobody, allegedly, where a choice changes an answer. M8's "for the text": a Terms act without `replaces` is a first version (that is the rule, say it in 5.4.1); the rest to M11's list." *Shaped*: encoding 3, section 11, "Open in this text, not yet chosen" (M10's edit to encoding 3); 5.4.1's first sentence (M10's edit (i)); M8's other "for the text" items are in the report's list for Nobody, allegedly. *The other reading* (M8's "Open", which lists the readings side by side): a byte choice made in encoding 3 by its writer. On the reading, none is made; for 5.4.1, the other reading is the rule left implicit in §4.2's "in every Terms act except the first", which a checker of one act cannot test (M8's "for the text", 2).
3. "M9's four for the text: all to M10, as you plan; 5.10.6 and 5.10.7 read together: no later update exists for the filling; each later version exists for the agreement." *Shaped*: 5.8.5 and 5.10.2 (M10's edit (h)); 5.10.6 (M10's edit (j)); §0 (M10's edit (k)); 5.13.3 (item 1 above). *The other reading* (M9's "for the text", 5): 5.10.6's "no later update exists" read as absolute, against 5.10.7's "each later version that changes what it earned". On the reading, a later version exists for the agreement, and changes what a filling earned only by its holder's own assent (5.10.7).

### 6.12 The reading of 15:34, on M10's "For M11"

*Added by M11, as the others are written: the lead's words, the rules each shaped, and the other reading.*

1. "Item 1: no rule beyond 5.7.2. Two assents of one signer that name the same previous fork her line, and neither counts until it is resolved. A second assent that names her first is in order: it is a knot, and U′ is shown after U wherever her assent is among the acts that put U through its gate (5.5.4); where it is not, and nothing else orders U and U′, they are a fork of the versions (5.12). Edit (a) reads so; the lead's 14:33 "forks her own line" meant 5.7.2's case. The lead's reading; your lean is not taken." *Shaped*: 5.5.5(a) and 5.7.2 (M11's edit (e), rereading M10's edit (a)). *The other reading* (the scribe's lean, not taken): a rule in 5.5.5(a), whatever `previous` each assent names: any two assents of one known party to two updates of one version would stop both assents for both gates. On it, a known party who assents to U and then, naming that assent, to U′, counts for neither; on the reading, the second assent counts, and U′ is late wherever her first assent helped put U through its gate.
2. "Item 2: the update condition's cMIP is given the assents that count: an assent in a tip that has stopped (5.7.2, 5.12.2) is not among them, and the verifier says which it left out and why. The lead's reading." *Shaped*: 5.5.7(a) (M11's edit (f)); K4's "Accepts" (§6) and 5.12.3's "One thing an assent to a tip still counts for", each given a pointer to 5.5.7(a) to agree (the scribe's, listed in the trace). *The other reading* (M10's "For M11", 2): the cMIP given every assent held, the forked ones included, and its answer read as it comes. On it, a named condition could count both forked assents; on the reading, it is given neither, and the verifier names them.
3. "Item 3: cite "The holder at the act cited" (17 November), and write "the envelope the contest names"." *Shaped*: 5.11.9 (M11's edit (i)); 6.8 row 2 above; section 9's row 5.11.9, which already cited it. *The other reading* (M10's edit (b)): "its own envelope". No verifier answer turns on the words.
4. "Item 4: a resolving update that resolves is an update of the shared act and takes an update's answers (valid; in force once pinned), 5.12.4 pointing to §5.5; 5.12.5 stands for one under a power." *Shaped*: 5.12.4 (M11's edit (g)). Written for a resolving update of Terms, since draft 26 says "valid" and "in force" of Terms only (§3.3); a resolve in one signer's line still has no answer word of its own when it resolves (listed). *The other reading* (M10's "For M11", 4; scenario 8.7): **shown**, the scribe's word, as 5.12.5 gives one under a power.
5. "Item 5: define "rival" in §3.3: each of two or more acts that compete to change the same state and that nothing orders; the tips of a fork." *Shaped*: §3.3 **Rival** (M11's edit (h)), used in 5.5.5(a) and §5.12 (5.12.8). *The other reading* (M10's "For M11", 5): "rival" left a plain word; the blind reader read two updates that fail the gate on her assents as perhaps no rivals at all.

### 6.13 Wave four: the lead's lean on four words, and what the five lines say beyond his words

*Added by N1, the scribe's session of wave four, as the others are written: the lead's words, the rules each shaped, and the other reading.*

1. The MOR lead's assignment of 27 November: "The four words used in two senses: one sense each, the other renamed (the lead's lean: "chain" for an agreement's versions only, "line" for a signer's acts, a keeper's "record"; "kind" for a Statement only, a spot's and a power's "sort"; "resolve" the Statement, "resolution" the fork's end; "in force" for Terms only, an act the keepers show is "placed")." *Shaped*: §3.3 **Chain** and **Resolution**; §3.2 **Spot** and **Power**; §3.4 **Shown after**, **Keeper**, **Keeper's record** and **Placed**; §4.1, §4.2, §4.4, 5.2.3, 5.4.1, 5.4.5, 5.5.1(b), 5.5.4(c), 5.5.5(d), 5.6.2, 5.6.5, 5.8.1, 5.8.4, 5.10.1, 5.10.4, 5.12.5, 5.13.3 and §6 (each place in the trace, N1). *The other reading* (M11's check 1, which listed each as a choice): the words left in two senses. No verifier answer turns on the renames (the scribe's reading), but for one: **placed** carries 5.8.3's every keeper, so an act is placed only once every keeper named shows it (answer 1 below).
2. The lines beyond his words, each the lead's, written as far as his words go:
   - "Two keepers named, both needed": his words, "both keepers are needed. Sorry can’t do"; the answer word "not shown", with that reason, is the line's. *Shaped*: 5.8.3, 5.8.4, 5.5.5(d), 5.6.4, §3.6.
   - "The known parties of a version are the signers it names": his "Yes" answers the MOR lead's "one rule, version by version"; "she was unknown to the versions before it" is the line's. *Shaped*: §3.2 **Known party**, 5.5.5(a), (b), 5.5.7(e). *The other reading*: "the signers it names", read strictly, would drop a party named as an agreement from the known parties, against the lead's reading of 09:47 (section 6.6); draft 26 keeps both, as far as the two agree.
   - ""Renewed on the terms then current" cannot be built": his "How did they build such terms in agreements?" and "Yes, it’s the scenario we trying to make difficult on the “walls of terms”"; "Renewal is a new assent … a new filling" and the client's "dial" are the MOR lead's, in the exchange and the line. The MOR lead's lean "her own deal" is withdrawn by the line, and with it, as far as it says she "buys that knowingly", the settled note's M28 (section 6.9). *Shaped*: no sentence of draft 26 (section 5, §5.10); scenario 8.24.
   - "The signer of a Terms act is an author": his "by default"; "invalid" is the line's. *Corrected*: the ledger's line now reads "such Terms are valid", and the lead's reading of 18:41 says "by default" "means without needing to be listed"; wave five follows it (section 6.15). *Shaped*: 5.3.1, §3.2 **Author**, §4.2, §4.4 case 5, encoding 3's E13. *The other reading*: "by default" as a default some Terms could set aside; draft 26 gives no field to do so.

### 6.15 The reading of 18:41, on "by default"

*Added by P2.* The MOR lead to the scribe, 27 November, 18:41, in the assignment of wave five: ""by default" means without needing to be listed. The signer of a Terms act is its first author always; `authors` names the others, and may name the signer too. ### 6.16 The readings of 20:41, for P2

*Added by P2.* The note is `docs/lead/notes/2026-11-27-readings-for-p2.md` on main, the MOR lead's, 27 November, 20:50: "The written form of the batch sent at 20:41: seven readings for the scribe, and two of the nine answers to the codemeister. ... Each reading below by the lead's number, with the scribe's question it answers (the scribe's batch of 19, numbered as P1's "For Nobody, allegedly"), the reading, what it rests on, and the other reading.

1. **The lead's 1, the scribe's 2**: which signers an update "needs"; "each shown after" (the P1 trace, For Nobody, 3). "The signers an update "needs" are those the condition's cMIP requires (K2's output); lacking one, by default, the known parties. "Each shown after" means every needed signer's act is shown after every needed assent." *Rests on*: "Known parties"; "In force once pinned" (18 November). *The other reading*: the needed signers are every known party of the original, whatever a named condition accepts, as the lead's turn in "A dead keeper" counts "a majority's update" among what a keeper orders; and "each shown after the other" read pair by pair (each signer's assent shown after the one before). The reading names K2's output for the condition's cMIP, which is K4's socket (§6): which of the two is meant is a question for the lead (the trace, P2). Scenarios 8.4 (variant) and 8.30.
2. **The lead's 2, the scribe's 1**: a first version pinned by knots (For Nobody, 4). "A first version that every party signs is pinned by their knots, whatever keeper is named." *Rests on*: "A dead keeper: an update every needed signer signs needs no keeper" (18:51). *The other reading*: 5.4.1 as P1 left it: where a first version names keepers, "only they pin"; the entry speaks of updates. Scenario 8.31.
3. **The lead's 3, the scribe's 3**: knots alone show lateness (For Nobody, 5). "With keepers named, knots alone still show lateness; a keeper vouches for the chaining only." *Rests on*: "Knots are knots"; "Two keepers named, both needed" (16:44). *The other reading*: 5.5.4(a) as P1 left it: "Where O names keepers, knots show L late only as a named keeper's contradiction". Scenario 8.32.
4. **The lead's 4, the scribe's 4**: Fable 1.1: the Terms act is its signer's assent (For Nobody, 6). "Fable 1.1: the Terms act is its signer's assent; the other parties assent later, each in an act of their own." *Rests on*: "The signer of a Terms act is an author" and its correction. *Marked by the lead for Nobody, allegedly, to see; seen by him at 20:50 (above).* *The other reading*: §4.3, **An author's first act**, 5.4.1 and 5.5.7(e) as P1 left them: the author's assent is a Statement apart, which names the Terms act in `previous`; without it the first version is **not shown** in force (Fable's answer, and the stranger's, 85%). The note marks this reading for Nobody, allegedly, to see; he has: the ledger's "Publishing the Terms is the publisher's assent" (27 November, 20:49–20:50), he: "Yes, I don’t see the need to have her sign twice". It is his now; draft 26's words for it are P3's. Scenario 8.33.

""In an act of her own" means a Statement in her own name, naming the grantee and the power's sort; K3 reads it." *Rests on*: "A filling gives no power"; the giver bears it ("Ana took the risk"). *The other reading*: any act of the holder's own (an assent in her name to a version that carries the power, say); or no act at all, a holder being an unknown party who "gives no power" (§3.2, **Two classes**), so that 5.6.1(b)'s "and they are a known party of that version" holds for her too. Scenario 8.34.
6. **The lead's 6, the scribe's 5**: a Terms act signed by a non-party (For Nobody, 7). "A Terms act signed by someone who is not a party is invalid." *Rests on*: "There is no such thing as an unsigned act"; "Known parties". *The other reading*: draft 26 as P1 left it: no case of 5.3.1 or §4.4 names it, and the act passes case 5 (its signer is the first author, unlisted, "whether or not `authors` names them"), §3.2 **Author** saying only that an author is named as a signer. Scenario 8.35; encoding 3's E13.
7. **The lead's 7, the scribe's 7**: a known party's own filling (For Nobody, 2). "A spot a known party fills is a known party's act: 5.10.7 (an unknown party's filling) does not stand for it." *Rests on*: "A filling gives no power", with "that fills an unknown party spot" and "these two are not the same class or IDs". *The other reading*: 5.10.7 as written ("A filling has exactly the rights offered by the version it was earned under", of any filling), read as standing for a known party's filling too, so that an update she did not sign changes what her filling earned only with her own assent. Scenario 8.36.

**For the codemeister**, two of the nine, which P2 uses: (1) "A keeper list that is present and empty is E18's fault." (2) "5.2.2 names the fault on a words note: the rule that names it is 5.2.2." *Shaped*: encoding 3's E18 and section 9, "One fault, one rule" (P2). *The other reading*: (1) an empty keeper list rejected under E6, the rule of the keeper's form; (2) a words note naming E25, the encoding's rule, or 5.13.3.

**The rest of the batch of 19, 20:55**, in the same note, the lead's readings of the scribe's 8 to 19, each "for P3 as the lead's, listed for him to check in the report". P2 carries none of them into the text; where encoding 3 would follow, it waits on draft 26's words: 8, 5.6.1(b)'s "in force" read at the act's own position (5.6.4); 9, what shows a signer's fork came after the update (knots or the keepers named; where nothing shows it, **not shown**, a needed signer's line forked); 10, a counter signature in a party's own name against an agent's assent (5.6.6(c)), and an agreement-party's pin (one act `for` it under a power its Terms give); 11, the principle in §0 and the reasons in this companion (P1-1 stands); 12, §3.2's **Two classes** gathers grammar rules and adds none; 13 and 14, "kind" and "sort" stand; 15, §4.4's heading "One fault, one grammar rule", "encoding 3 follows" (encoding 3's section 9 keeps "One fault, one rule" until draft 26's heading changes: the trace, P2, "For P3"); 16, K6 keeps "Control"; 17, §3.1 **Rule**'s "it trusts nobody" rewritten; 18, each sentence said once; 19, three wordings (5.4.2, 5.4.5, 5.11.9). And, of his 20:50: "§4.3's paragraph **An author's first act** ... and every rule that counts the author's assent apart (5.4.1, 5.5.7(e)) change with it ... P3 writes it; encoding 3 and the vectors follow." Encoding 3's E17 carries a note of it (the trace, P2).

---

## 7. The stated costs

**In plain words.** A stated cost is a case the grammar does not fit, said plainly: what a rule trades away, and who carries it. None is closed by a grammar rule. Draft 25 kept thirty-eight of them in its own section 7; draft 26 keeps only rules ("Only rules in the hashed text", 17 November: "Yes, the text should be just that. Everything else goes into companion documents for the few who might be interested."), so they are here, each marked as its rule went.

**How to read it.** Each cost has its draft 25 title, then a mark: *kept* (its rule stayed, and draft 25's words stand, quoted unchanged, its rule numbers draft 25's); *reworded* (its rule changed, and the words are the scribe's, in draft 26's terms, saying what the cost now is and nothing more; draft 25's words stay in draft 25); *left with its rule* (its rule moved to another text, and the cost with it, quoted unchanged here for the record); *dropped* (its rule went). Then the draft 26 rules it concerns, and the ledger entry that reaches it, where one does (the outline's item 7 names them; the sort of each cost is the scribe's reading). Where a kept cost has a word that changed (place, keeper), the change is said in one line.

### 7.1 Draft 25's thirty-eight

**1. Minds.** *Kept.* Draft 26: 5.3.2; §6.1 (Assurance).

> Whether a party was able (#3) or free (#5) cannot be shown by any act. Assurance (section 5.3) can make it more likely and record what was done; nothing assigns its failure. Only Align is assigned (G3).

**2. Conceal (#39).** *Kept.* Draft 26: §3.5 (Lodge); 5.8.2.

> Terms can be kept private and their fingerprint lodged in a place; nothing can show the words were not told to someone, and telling cannot be undone.

"lodged in a place" now reads "lodged at a relay" (§3.5); nothing else changes.

**3. Enforce beyond what was posted (#45).** *Kept.* Draft 26: §5.7 (a signer's line, D25's G6); §6.1 (Standing).

> Words can order a release; something must obey. Against value not under Control, force lies outside the protocol. A proven liar (G6) loses only what they posted and their standing with those who see the proof (standing, section 5.3). Against a stranger who vanishes, the proof is worth nothing.

**4. Deeds lean on witnesses.** *Kept.* Draft 26: §6.1 (Witness).

> The more an agreement is about services and acts in the world instead of value under Control, the more it rests on witnesses (section 5.3), whose statements prove only that the witness said them.

**5. Spots lean on their confirmers.** *Reworded.* Draft 26: 5.10.1, 5.10.2; K5 (§6). Reached by "How a reader answers goes to the MICS" (18 November), which moves G8.4.

Each kind of spot has its own recorded fraud (`late-roles.md`): doing is faked "by simulating the act or stealing credit"; receiving "by forging the chain"; holding "by forging the object or issuing more than exists"; being "by false or multiplied identities". Draft 26 fixes the test in advance (5.10.1) and answers each filling with its proof, each act reported by who signed it (5.10.2, the MICS); neither makes the confirmer honest.

**6. Future spots are the authors' word.** *Kept.* Draft 26: 5.10.4, 5.3.2.

> Whether a future spot was used only for participants no one could anticipate cannot be checked (G10.4). Authors who use one for an ordinary participant, to escape the four kinds' clearer tests, are not stopped by the grammar.

**7. Counted attention leans on whoever counts, and the grammar names no one.** *Kept.* Draft 26: K5 (§6); section 4.3 of this companion (draft 25's §5.1).

> Being seen is counted by whoever a spot's cMIP names (section 5.1). The grammar itself no longer requires an agreed witness: an agreement whose cMIP names none, or names the payer, has counts that rest on whoever states them, and inflated attention (`late-roles.md`: "Roles counted by attention suffered fabrication of the event itself") is then exposed only as far as that cMIP goes. A rule that counts only what led to a settlement leaves out someone truly seen whom no payment yet stands behind; a rule that rewards pure attention rests every count on the one who counts.

**8. "Now" is what was read, and no more.** *Reworded.* Draft 26: 5.13.2, 5.1.4, 5.5.3, 5.13.5. Reached by "Whose place counts; protect the unknown party, as a principle" (26 November), with the lead's reading on G1.4 (section 6.3).

Under 5.13.2, a payer who names the version they read has paid as that version says, even if the agreement had already changed without their being able to know. That protects the payer; it also means the owners under a later version can see money go by an earlier one. Whether the version named was still the last is answered only per the keepers the Terms name, or as shown (5.5.3, 5.13.5): a reader's own anchoring gives only that reader's answer. The climb up a sequence gets less certain at each agreement that names no keeper.

**9. A work that points nowhere cannot be paid.** *Kept.* Draft 26: 5.1.4.

> Under G1, what is owed to an agreement whose version names no receiver has nowhere to go, and is not shown.

**10. A deal is as complete as its words.** *Kept.* Draft 26: §3.2 (Service, deal); K6.

> Who bears a service's fee, and what becomes of a payment that arrives after the work has named another receiver, are whatever the deal states, read by its cMIP. A deal that says nothing on them leaves them unsaid; the grammar supplies no answer.

**11. A service is a hand.** *Reworded.* Draft 26: 5.11.3, K6 (§6). Reached by "Where three rules live" (17 November), with the lead's reading on G11.3 and K6 (section 6.3).

Nothing in the grammar makes a service divide as the shares say, or pay at all. Draft 26 asks of a leg report what it carries: what was received, passed on, kept and still owed (K6's row), and only where the deal called for it (5.11.2, 5.11.3). How two reports that state different amounts for one settlement are read (draft 25's G11.4(c): shown side by side, neither chosen) is now the MICS's report block's. Getting the money is another matter ("Enforce beyond what was posted"). An owner who receives for the others is a hand in the same way (5.11.2): nothing makes them pass it on.

**12. A sequence is readable only as far as its agreements call for reports, and are answered.** *Kept.* Draft 26: 5.11.2, 5.11.4, 5.11.7.

> G11 makes no Terms carry a call for reports, and makes nobody answer a call. From a leg whose call is unanswered, or which has none, nothing ties what lies beyond it to the click: the people beyond are not shown, though they never chose that leg (in the click test, one silent service hid a producer and a whole band). And a call binds only the signers of the Terms that carry it: a work cannot make the works it stands on, or their services, report. What a buyer has is this: before paying, which agreements carry a call for reports, and from whom, read from their signed Terms; afterwards, the receiving side's confirmation of their own payment, and then access to what they bought (G11.7). Which further calls were answered is seen by the two sides of each leg. And a report is its signer's word: it makes the whole readable, not true.

**13. One leg, and no further.** *Kept.* Draft 26: 5.11.5, 5.11.6, 5.11.7; K8.

> By the grammar each report goes to the other side of its own leg, and nowhere else (G11.6). The buyer is shown that their payment was received, and by default no more (G11.7): not whether the money reached every member. The buyer's own leg is read beyond it only as the data cMIP of the offer the buyer signed says (G11.6(b)): a buyer who signs an offer whose Terms name one has agreed to it, as any signer has. And nothing makes the receiving side confirm: a payment nobody confirmed is shown as one side only. A buyer, or a partner, who wants to know whether a split among people at the far end was carried out reads it only where the people of every leg on the way agreed, with their signature, to a data cMIP that gives it. No call gives it. Terms name such a cMIP in `data` (K8), and this text does not say what it is beyond that socket (section 8.4): until one is written and signed for, nobody reads further than their own leg. Where one leg did not agree, the far end is not shown to that reader, whatever was reported there; and the one who received is the only one who says it was passed on. That is the price of not making surveillance cheap. The other side of it: where the people of a leg did sign for such a cMIP, they are read as far as they signed; and where they signed a rule by which the majority decides, a member who was outvoted is read as the majority decided. The grammar reads their signature, not whether they were free to refuse it ("Minds").

Draft 25's "(section 8.4)" is its open list, now in section 11 of this companion; draft 25's G11.6 ("each report goes to the other side of its own leg") is the MICS's (§3.1, Report).

**14. A contest reaches one agreement.** *Kept.* Draft 26: 5.11.8.

> A buyer who paid and was not delivered to contests the signers of the offer or vow they bought under, and nobody else (G11.8). If the fault lies with a service those signers picked, the buyer depends on them to contest the service in turn; nothing makes them, and the buyer is not shown whether they did. A contest is a word on the record, the buyer's; it moves no money.

**15. Paid, and the author gone.** *Kept.* Draft 26: 5.11.8; §6.1 (Standing).

> An open offer whose only author goes absent, with nothing that delivers by itself: whoever paid and got nothing can contest. The contest reaches the signers of the agreement they bought under (G11.8), and here that is the author, who is gone. It stays on the record for anyone to read (standing, section 5.3). The record cannot give the money back, since nobody is left to sign. A buyer who wants that pays through an escrow service: a hand, under its deal, for a fee. Without one, the loss is the buyer's. Nothing in the grammar prevents an author from vanishing with what was paid; that is dealt with, if at all, in how services are built. In the words of Nobody, allegedly: "MOR only offers ways to ring the alarm", and "MOR just tracks receipts".

**16. Order without an anchoring is partial.** *Reworded.* Draft 26: 5.13.3, 5.13.4, 5.13.5, 5.5.4, 5.8.5. Reached by "Knots are knots: one reading, "shown after"" (27 November); "Whose place counts; protect the unknown party, as a principle" (26 November).

Naming shows only "after" (5.13.3, 5.13.4). Two acts that never name each other have no order from the acts alone, and nothing shows "nothing later". Where an agreement names no keeper, its order comes from knots alone: a reader that accepts an anchoring of its own gets an answer only as that reader's, named so, never the Terms' (5.5.3, 5.13.5). So a late update is seen as late only as far as the acts name one another (5.5.4(a)): the bytes alone cannot tell late from simultaneous, and a late update whose signers named nothing that shows the replacement is a fork. Where the Terms name keepers and a clock, every reader uses them (5.8.5), and the answers are as good as those keepers and that clock.

**17. Valid is as sure as the update condition's cMIP.** *Reworded.* Draft 26: 5.5.7(c), 5.5.7(h), 5.5.7(i), 5.8.6, 5.10.6, 5.10.8. Reached by "A condition in time with no anchor is refused" (26 November); "Ana's month ends by the clock the agreement names" and "For the unknown party there is no update, only the agreement she signed" (27 November).

An update condition that reads someone's statement rests on whoever made the statement (the MICS); one that reads a time rests on the clock the Terms name (5.5.7(c)), and Terms that do not supply what the condition's cMIP declares it needs (a clock, a keeper) are invalid (5.8.6): no reader's anchoring answers for them. A reader who lacks that cMIP cannot tell whether any update passed the gate: from there on, which Terms are in force is unchecked for them (5.5.7(h)). A condition is as wide or as narrow as people wrote it: one that answers valid for everything protects no part of the agreement, and one that answers valid for nothing leaves an agreement that even all its parties together cannot update (5.5.7(i)). What changed: a filling is no longer at the update condition's mercy. No update reaches it (5.10.6); what it gave lasts as long as its version's words say, on the clock that version names, as the cMIP that reads the words answers (5.10.8). Its cost is the next one's ("Bound by an update one did not sign") and N5's, below.

**18. Reputation is as wide as the acts one holds.** *Kept.* Draft 26: §6.1 (Standing); 5.12.2.

> Standing (section 5.3) answers only from what the one asking holds, per place or as shown. Two people holding different acts compute different standing for the same signer, and honest forks (below) count against honest people.

"per place" reads "per keeper".

**19. Without a place, no scarcity and no freshness.** *Reworded.* Draft 26: 5.8.1, 5.8.4, 5.13.5. Reached by "The place vouches for the chaining only" (26 November); "How a reader answers goes to the MICS" (18 November), which moves G7.3.

Without a keeper, no scarcity and no freshness. The same thing can be given twice in two branches, the latest act can be withheld, silence cannot be counted. Draft 25's G7.3 (every such answer qualified as shown where no place is named) made this visible; its qualifier is now §3.6's **as shown**, and the rule itself the MICS's. Naming a keeper does not cure it either: a keeper vouches for the chaining only (5.8.1), not for what was left out of it.

**20. Ownership is as wide as its place.** *Reworded.* Draft 26: 5.8.1; 5.11.9; Envelopes (G14). Reached by "The place vouches for the chaining only" (26 November); "Where three rules live" and "The holder at the act cited" (17 November).

Ownership is as wide as its keeper. Two parties who accept different keepers cannot settle who holds a thing between them. The same thing sold in two separate agreements is seen by neither agreement's rules. What draft 25 said here of a baked work (Terms for an envelope count only with the holder of the baker's rights at the act cited; a second sale of the baker's rights a fork in the envelope's chain; a late Terms citing a point before a transfer) rests on G14, which now is Envelopes' (§2, `envelope_at`); its text stays in draft 25, and is listed for Envelopes below.

**21. The baker is as the envelope says.** *Left with its rule.* Draft 26: Envelopes (G14.4); 5.11.9. Reached by "Where three rules live" (17 November).

> G14 rests on the envelope and on whoever it names as baker, as the core gives them. It does not stop anyone from editing a work and baking it again under their own name: that is another envelope, and whether it owes anything to the first is not read by this grammar. It reads only Terms that name an envelope (P18).

Its rule left for Envelopes with the baker's rules; listed there, below.

**22. A copy is made legible, not forbidden.** *Left with its rule.* Draft 26: Envelopes (G14.5); 5.11.9. Reached by "Where three rules live" (17 November).

> Anyone may bake the same bytes again, unedited, and sell them under Terms of their own: those Terms count (G14.5). The bytes say who uploaded and when, not who made it, so the grammar never picks between bakes of the same work. A buyer is shown the other bakes only as far as the verifier holds them. Which is the original is a ranking a client may make, a judgment a cMIP or an arbiter may give, and a contest its maker may raise; none of them is a verifier's answer, and a contest moves no money.

Its rule left for Envelopes with the baker's rules; listed there, below.

**23. Closed world hurts the one who did not lodge.** *Reworded.* Draft 26: 5.8.4, 5.7.2. Reached by "The place vouches for the chaining only" (26 November); the lead's reading of 10:07 (section 6.7).

Under 5.8.4, where the Terms name keepers, a party's own act that the keepers never show counts for no answer about order, even if it is genuine; an act the keepers do not yet show is not yet placed (5.4.1, 5.5.1(b), §3.4). It still counts as a tip of a fork in its signer's own line (5.7.2): hiding an act from the keepers does not hide a fork.

**24. Honest forks.** *Kept.* Draft 26: 5.7.2, 5.12.3, 5.12.4, 5.12.6. Reached by "A fork is a fork" (27 November).

> A signer who loses track of their last act (a lost device, a restored backup) and signs again produces a fork that looks exactly like a lie. Honest drift and lying look the same from outside. Both tips stop, with everything that signer built on either, until the signer resolves the fork (G12.3), if they still hold their key; what others relied on in the tip set aside stands, shown as disputed (G12.5).

**25. A fork of Terms holds the agreement where it was.** *Reworded.* Draft 26: 5.5.4, 5.5.4(e), 5.5.5(a), 5.12.3, 5.12.5, 5.12.8. Reached by "A fork is a fork" (27 November); "The gate and the pin; a fork, not a race" (26 November); the lead's reading of 14:33 (section 6.10).

While two updates of the same Terms both stand, the Terms before them stay in force: neither tip's change takes effect, including a new receiver (5.1.4), and nothing built on either counts (5.12.3). If the agreement gave no power to resolve (5.12.5), and those whose signatures a resolving update needs will not sign one, it stays there; the grammar shows it and picks no tip. Where the update condition lets fewer than all update the Terms, any group it accepts can hold the agreement where it was: by signing a rival update, and then, against a resolving update, a rival one (5.12.8). A fork is then a veto; the lead called it the forced deadlock, "to push them to resolve it" (the lead's line). One signer alone cannot make it: a known party who assents to both rivals forks their own line, and neither assent counts for either gate until that line is resolved (5.5.5(a), 5.7.2; the lead's reading of 14:33). It can reach back: a rival to a version long since replaced, which the version's update condition answers valid, is a fork where nothing shows that it came after that version was replaced (per keeper, or by knots), and every version made since then stops with the first tip (5.5.4). Where it is shown late, it replaces nothing. The way out can be said in the language: a power to resolve whose rule accepts only the one resolving update it computes from the acts and the keepers' answers (5.12.5). That is a cMIP's.

**26. Resolving does not undo.** *Kept.* Draft 26: 5.12.6.

> What others relied on in a tip set aside stands, shown as disputed (G12.5). A resolving update settles which tip goes on; it cannot take back what a stranger did relying on the other.

**27. Withdrawing before in force.** *Reworded.* Draft 26: 5.4.1, 5.4.5, 5.12.2. Reached by "In force once pinned" (18 November); "A fork is a fork" (27 November); the ruling on item 1 and the lead's reading of 10:07 (sections 6.1, 6.7).

Between a first assent and the last, the first signer holds an open option against themselves. They can close it: an assent may be cancelled, by a counter signature, until the assents that put the Terms in force are shown before it (5.4.5). Where nothing orders the counter signature against the completing assent, the two are a fork: neither takes effect, and the Terms are not in force until it is resolved (5.4.5, 5.12.2). Only keepers the Terms name, or an `in_force` rule with a deadline read from a keeper or a clock the Terms name, settle it in advance. Once the Terms are pinned (5.4.1), no counter signature changes anything ("no signature taken back after it", the lead's line of 18 November).

**28. An offer that stops taking new people.** *Reworded.* Draft 26: 5.10.5, 5.10.6, 5.10.7. Reached by "For the unknown party there is no update, only the agreement she signed" (27 November); item 29 (left out).

The same question, between whatever stops the offer (a new version coming into force, a date, the hundredth buyer) and a stranger's filling the spot. Who came in time is answered by the spot's cMIP, per the keepers and the clock the Terms name, and without them only as shown (5.10.5); where the two are not ordered, the order is not shown. Whatever that answer, a filling already earned keeps its version (5.10.6, 5.10.7). What the known parties may still do to the offer for newcomers (the line's "close the offer, or dissolve it among themselves") is item 29, left out.

**29. Secret votes need a teller.** *Kept.* Draft 26: §6.1 (Tally).

> A tally (section 5.3) requires trusting whoever counts.

**30. Identity ends in acquaintance.** *Kept.* Draft 26: §6.1 (Identity); 5.10.1 (fits).

> Who vouches for the voucher (identity, section 5.3) ends in someone knowing someone; the grammar cannot express that. Spots of kind fits rest on it.

**31. The record holds no value.** *Kept.* Draft 26: §6.1 (Witness); K6.

> Every act about value is a claim or an instruction. The gap between "the record says 100" and "there are 60" must be checked continuously (a witness, section 5.3; K6), not once.

**32. Those who cannot sign.** *Kept.* Draft 26: §3.2 (Party).

> A contributor who has no key (the long dead, the unknowing) can be named in words but is no party, and nothing can arrive for them.

**33. No default way out.** *Reworded.* Draft 26: 5.5.5(a), 5.5.6, 5.5.7(e). Reached by "Who signs an update by default: the known parties" (26 November); "Delegation is a leftover" (18 November).

By 5.5.7(e), an agreement that names no update condition changes only by the assent of every known party. And since no agreement ends (5.5.6), none can be left by ending it: the last version keeps binding until those its Terms require sign a new one. If a known party is gone and no successor was named in advance (#37, #38), it is stuck. The way out is designed in advance or not at all: for a party who wants to leave, an update condition that answers valid for an update that party alone signed, only where it takes its own signer out (section 4.3 of this companion). Draft 25's one exception (a delegation ends by its delegator alone, G5.5) goes with delegation. A filling is not stuck in the same way: no update reaches it, and what it gave lasts only as its version's words say (5.10.6, 5.10.8).

**34. A judge is as hard to change as the Terms.** *Kept.* Draft 26: 5.6.5(d).

> A judge named in the Terms, and a power to resolve, change only by an update (G5.6). Where the update condition asks everyone, and the judge is gone, or one party will not sign, the judge stays named and nothing a power does can put another in their place. People who want a judge that can be replaced say how in their update condition, in advance.

**35. Bound by an update one did not sign.** *Reworded.* Draft 26: 5.6.1(c), 5.6.2, 5.10.6. Reached by "Who signs an update by default: the known parties" (26 November); "For the unknown party there is no update, only the agreement she signed" (27 November).

Where Terms name an update condition that fewer than all can meet, a known party is bound by an update it never signed (5.6.1(c)). It signed for that when it signed Terms that name the condition; the grammar shows the condition's fingerprint, and reads nothing of whether that party understood it ("Minds"). No longer a filling: whoever holds one is bound by the version its proof names and by no update (5.10.6, N5).

**36. Ordering and lines show patterns.** *Kept.* Draft 26: §5.7, §5.8; §6.1 (Standing).

> G6 and G7 make the shape of an agreement (who acted, how often, in what order) visible to whoever sees the acts or the place, even when the words are concealed. Standing (section 5.3) reads exactly those patterns.

"the place" reads "the keepers".

**37. What an agent signed stays signed.** *Kept.* Draft 26: 5.6.6. Reached by "Delegation is a leftover" (18 November).

> A grantor who regrets an agent's act cannot set it aside (G5.7): once the Terms are in force, it changes what was made only by an update, which those who agreed to it may have to sign, as the update condition asks. Whoever hands an agent a power carries what the agent made with it.

**38. An agent has what was signed, and no more.** *Dropped.* Draft 26: none: G5.5 goes. Reached by "Delegation is a leftover" (18 November).

Draft 25: "By default a delegator ends a delegation at any time, and the grammar gives the agent no notice and no pay (G5.5)." Draft 26 has no delegation and no delegator's default ("Delegation is a leftover", 18 November: "I think it's best. It's a leftover of the old ways"); an agent holds a power, which ends as the power's own rule or a version of its Terms says (5.6.6(a)). Its words, with his "we’re not Law.", stay in draft 25.

COUNTED (`costs10.py`, M10's scratch script): 38 costs, as in draft 25's section 7: kept 22, reworded 13, left with its rule 2, dropped 1.

### 7.2 The costs of the new rules

One line each. Where a line of the ledger gives the cost, it is quoted and labelled (his, or the lead's); where none does, the line is the scribe's.

- **N1, the gate and the pin (5.5.1).** One more round of signatures for a bare agreement: an update, like a first version, counts only once its order is shown, and where the Terms name no keeper that means every needed signer acting again after the completion. *Source*: the lead's, "In force once pinned" (18 November): "one more round of signatures for a bare agreement".
- **N2, the keeper, one or several (5.8.1 to 5.8.4).** Two keepers, two dependencies: where they disagree there is no order ("not shown"), a forced deadlock like a fork. *Source*: the lead's, "The place vouches for the chaining only" (26 November, 17:49): "the cost, two dependencies".
- **N3, the set of acts (§4.1).** A set's value names no act a verifier can read without the list it was computed from: it shows nothing of order, completeness or meaning, and anyone holding only the value learns nothing from it. *Source*: the scribe's, from §4.1.
- **N4, the words are a Text act (5.2.2).** One more act per agreement: the words are lodged first, as a Text act, and the Terms name it; where the Text act's signer later leaves it out by a change of key, only those who relied on it keep it standing (N10). *Source*: the lead's, "The words of Terms are a Text act" (26 November).
- **N5, no update reaches a filling (5.10.6).** The seller is bound to each filling for as long as its version's words say: changing the deal for those already in is not possible by update. *Source*: his, "Ana's month ends by the clock the agreement names" (27 November, 07:53): "It’s a problem that really only impacts subscriptions models I think".
- **N6, how long, on the version's clock (5.10.8).** A filling whose version names no clock has no term in time that can be read from it (5.8.6); one whose words state no term has none a verifier can show (item 11, left out). *Source*: the scribe's, from 5.10.8 and item 11.
- **N7, "shown after" (5.13.3).** The stall: where an act a chain needs is named and not held, the answer stays unchecked, with no door around it; a keeper prevents it in advance. *Source*: the lead's, "A missing act stays unchecked: no door around it" (26 November): "The stall is the forced deadlock: only someone the update condition accepts can make it, the agreement resolves it as a fork, and a keeper prevents it in advance".
- **N8, a fork is a fork (5.12.1).** One signer's withdrawal against a completing assent stops the Terms from coming into force until the fork is resolved, and draft 26 gives no form for resolving that fork in bytes (item 7). *Source*: his, "What a race stops" (18 November): "Agreed, Carla is the monkey wrench that gets it all stuck"; the gap is the scribe's, from item 7.
- **N9, no stated anchor, no condition in time (5.8.6).** Where only the words speak of a time, no verifier answers about it: a seller who sells time names a clock, and tools that check words against fields are needed above the core. *Source*: his, "A condition in time with no anchor is refused" (26 November): "Tools that verify that text and agreement state the same thing will be needed".

Draft 26's keeper costs one more thing, said by none of these: Terms that name keepers are in force only once those keepers show them, so a keeper that stops answering stops the agreement's order (5.4.1, 5.8.4). The scribe's, from 5.8.4.

## 8. The scenarios

**In plain words.** Each scenario is a short story told in acts, in the order they are signed, with what a verifier answers under draft 26 at each step and which rule gives it. A verifier can be run on each: the acts are the input, the answers the expected output. Where an answer turns on something draft 26 leaves out, the scenario is told up to that point and says where it stops; nothing is chosen.

**How to read it.** *V1, V2, V3* are versions (Terms acts) of one agreement; *U1, U2* updates of one version; *A(x, V)* is x's assent to V; *CS(x)* x's counter signature. "Names" means a fingerprint in a field (a knot, 5.13.1). Each answer is given with its word in bold, its rule, and its qualifier (**as shown**, **per keeper**, **per clock**). Unless a scenario says otherwise: the Terms name no keeper, no clock and no update condition; the parties named as signers are the known parties (§3.2); each assent names in `previous` its signer's last act within the agreement (5.7.1); the verifier holds every act listed and every cMIP named. The names (Ana, Ben, Carla) are the ledger's. The expected answers are the scribe's reading of draft 26's rules (INFERRED, each with its rules); none is a vector, and none changes one.

The scenarios are of three sources: the ledger's (8.1 to 8.7), the impact sheet's twenty-two new vectors by its line (8.8 to 8.16), and the five blind readers' cases, M4 to M8 (8.17 to 8.21). The impact sheet was written before draft 26 and covers the exchanges up to "Wave two: check, then attack, then draft 26" (26 November); each of its expected answers is re-read here against draft 26, and where draft 26's answer differs from the sheet's, both are given.

### 8.1 Ana, Ben and Carla: a withdrawal against the completing assent

*Source*: "What a race stops" (18 November: "Ben's signature completing Terms while Carla takes back hers, with nothing ordering the two"; he: "Agreed, Carla is the monkey wrench that gets it all stuck"); "In force once pinned" (18 November); "A fork is a fork" (27 November). The ledger records that the MOR lead "retold it with Ana, Ben and Carla" in "The gate and the pin; a fork, not a race" (26 November); the retelling's own acts are not in the ledger, so the acts below are the 18 November case's.

*Acts*: (1) V1, a first version: parties Ana, Ben and Carla, each named as a signer; author Ana; no `in_force`. (2) A(Ana, V1). (3) A(Carla, V1). (4) A(Ben, V1), naming none of Carla's acts. (5) CS(Carla), `terms` V1, its `previous` naming (3); it names nothing of Ben's.

*Answers*:
- After (4): the assents V1's default asks for are present (5.4.1, first sentence: every party named as a signer); V1 is not yet in force: **not shown**, no order being shown, since no party has acted after the completion (5.4.1, the pin), **as shown**.
- After (5): with no keeper, only knots order (5.4.5, The order). (5) is not shown after (4), so it is not late. Counting every act but those shown after (5), (4) is still counted and V1 would come into force, so (5) is not shown to have come first. With every act counted, V1 would come into force. So (5) and the completion are a **fork** (5.4.5; 5.12.2: "Terms coming into force against a counter signature"): neither takes effect, V1 is not in force, and the verifier shows both and chooses neither (5.12.1).
- *Variant*: (5) names (4) (Carla had seen Ben's assent). Then (5) is shown after the completion: it is **late**, cancels nothing, and pins for Carla (5.4.1: "a counter signature so shown is late, and cancels nothing"); V1 is in force once Ana and Ben have also acted after the completion (5.4.1), **as shown**.
- *Variant*: V1 names a keeper K. Then K's positions settle it: (5) changes nothing where K places it after the completion, and cancels where before (5.4.5, The order), **per keeper**; V1 is in force once K shows the completing assents (5.4.1).

*Stops*: how the fork of step (5) is resolved in bytes is not given: 5.12.4 gives the form of a resolving update "for a fork of a line only" (item 7, left out). Whether Carla's counter signature cancels once such a fork is resolved is "as the resolver decides" (the lead's reading of 09:47, section 6.6, item 6); draft 26 has no rule for it.

### 8.2 Ana's month

*Source*: "Ana's month ends by the clock the agreement names" (27 November); "For the unknown party there is no update, only the agreement she signed" (27 November); "A buyer under a replaced version: what version 2 says" (26 November: "If she bought for a month of service to an LLM for example, the deal is valid a month.").

*Acts*: (1) V1, a first version by the seller S, named as a signer and author; a role (say of kind fits, its `what` and its K5 cMIP); `in_force` (required, 5.4.2); `clock` C; a K1 cMIP whose words give a buyer one month. (2) A(S, V1), and acts that put V1 in force and pin it (5.4.1). (3) A(Ana, V1), which earns a filling by the K5 cMIP's proof. (4) V2, `replaces` V1, signed by S alone, naming clock C2 and dropping the role. (5) Acts of S that pin V2.

*Answers*:
